```json
  "id": "Tsovinar adalah dewi air, laut, dan hujan dalam mitologi Armenia, dewi yang garang yang memaksa hujan turun dari langit dengan amarahnya.",
  "en": "Tsovinar is the Armenian goddess of water, sea and rain, a fierce goddess who forced rain from the sky with her fury.",

 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Tsovinar, juga disebut Nar, adalah dewi air, laut, dan hujan dalam mitologi Armenia. Ia dewi yang garang, yang dengan amarahnya memaksa hujan turun dari langit. Dalam mitologi Armenia kuno ia juga perwujudan badai dan petir, menunggang kuda api di awan saat badai, dan mengirim hujan yang menghidupkan atau hujan es yang merusak.",
   "en": "Tsovinar, also called Nar, is the water, sea and rain goddess of Armenian myth. A fierce deity, she drove the rain down from the sky with her rage. Ancient Armenian myth also makes her the embodiment of storm and lightning, riding a fiery horse through the clouds in a storm and sending either life-giving rain or ruinous hail.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "tsovinar-c01",
    "tsovinar-c04"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Namanya berarti \"putri lautan\", dan dalam tradisi epos Armenia ia ibu Sanasar dan Baghdasar. Dalam epos Armenia ia juga putri Raja Gagik dan istri Khalifah Baghdad, yang mengandung si kembar dari dua genggam air laut yang ia minum. Saat kemarau, orang mengadakan arak-arakan dan nyanyian ritual untuknya agar hujan turun ke ladang.",
   "en": "Her name means \"daughter of the seas\", and in Armenian epic tradition she is the mother of Sanasar and Baghdasar. In Armenian epic she is also King Gagik's daughter and the Caliph of Baghdad's wife, who conceived the twins from two handfuls of sea water she drank. In droughts people held processions and ritual songs for her, asking rain for the fields.",

 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "tsovinar-c01",
   "source_id": "tsovinar-s1",
   "quote": "or Nar (Նար) was the Armenian goddess of water, sea, and rain. She was a fierce goddess, who forced the rain to fall from the sky with her fury.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Tsovinar (Ծովինար) atau Nar adalah dewi air, laut, dan hujan Armenia, dewi garang yang memaksa hujan turun dari langit dengan amarahnya.",
    "en": "Tsovinar or Nar was the Armenian goddess of water, sea and rain, a fierce goddess who forced rain from the sky with her fury."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "tsovinar-c02",
   "source_id": "tsovinar-s1",
   "quote": "Her name, Tzovinar, means \"daughter of the seas\" and she is identified as the mother of Sanasar and Baghdasar in Armenian epic tradition.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Namanya berarti \"putri lautan\", dan dalam tradisi epos Armenia ia ibu Sanasar dan Baghdasar.",
    "en": "Her name means \"daughter of the seas\", and in Armenian epic tradition she is the mother of Sanasar and Baghdasar."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "tsovinar-c03",
   "source_id": "tsovinar-s1",
   "quote": "Her name can be decomposed into two parts: Armenian/Old Armenian tzov 'sea, large body of water', and nar or Nar.",
   "locator": "Name and etymology",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Namanya terdiri atas dua bagian: tzov dalam bahasa Armenia, \"laut, perairan besar\", dan nar atau Nar.",
    "en": "Her name has two parts: Armenian tzov, \"sea, large body of water\", and nar or Nar."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "tsovinar-c04",
   "source_id": "tsovinar-s2",
   "quote": "богиня в древнеармянской мифологии, персонификация грозы, молнии, и одновременно богиня моря, воды, дождя, во время грозы скачущая на огненном коне в облаках; она посылает людям живительный дождь или вредносный град.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Tsovinar, juga Tsovyan, adalah dewi dalam mitologi Armenia kuno, perwujudan badai dan petir sekaligus dewi laut, air, dan hujan, yang saat badai menunggang kuda api di awan dan mengirim hujan yang menghidupkan atau hujan es yang merusak.",
    "en": "Tsovinar, also Tsovyan, is a goddess of ancient Armenian mythology, personification of storm and lightning and goddess of sea, water and rain, riding a fiery horse in the clouds during storms and sending life-giving rain or harmful hail."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "tsovinar-c05",
   "source_id": "tsovinar-s2",
   "quote": "Во время засухи ей посвящались шествия и ритуальные песнопения с пожеланиями послать полям дождь.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Saat kemarau, arak-arakan dan nyanyian ritual dipersembahkan kepadanya dengan permohonan agar hujan turun ke ladang.",
    "en": "In droughts, processions and ritual songs were dedicated to her asking for rain on the fields."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "tsovinar-c06",
   "source_id": "tsovinar-s2",
   "quote": "Цовинар —  дочь царя Гагика, жена Багдадского Халифа, мать первых сасунских героев близнецов Санасара и Багдасара, зачатых ею от выпитых ею двух пригоршней морской воды.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Tsovinar adalah putri Raja Gagik, istri Khalifah Baghdad, dan ibu si kembar Sanasar dan Baghdasar yang ia kandung dari dua genggam air laut yang ia minum.",
    "en": "Tsovinar is King Gagik's daughter, the Caliph of Baghdad's wife and mother of the twins Sanasar and Baghdasar, conceived from two handfuls of sea water she drank."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "tsovinar-c07",
   "source_id": "tsovinar-s2",
   "quote": "В 1973 году композитор Григор Ахинян написал оперу «Цовинар».",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "modern-popular-culture",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Pada 1973 komposer Grigor Akhinian menulis opera \"Tsovinar\".",
    "en": "In 1973 composer Grigor Akhinian wrote the opera \"Tsovinar\"."
   }
  }
 ],
```

```json
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Zmei (\"ular\") adalah bentuk naga dalam dongeng dan epos Rusia, kadang tokoh mirip manusia bersifat naga; yang terkenal antara lain Gorynych dan Tugarin.",
  "en": "Zmei (\"serpent\") is a form of dragon in Russian folk tales and epics, sometimes a human-like figure with dragon traits; famous ones include Gorynych and Tugarin.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "zmei-russian-folklore-c01",
   "zmei-russian-folklore-c02"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Zmei (laki-laki) dan zmeya (perempuan), yang berarti \"ular\", adalah bentuk naga dalam dongeng dan epos Rusia, kadang tokoh mirip manusia yang bersifat naga. Dua zmei paling terkenal adalah Gorynych dan Tugarin, lawan para bogatyr Dobrynya Nikitich dan Alyosha Popovich. Walau berarti ular, istilah zmei untuk naga di seluruh Rusia selalu merujuk pada jenis yang terbang.",
   "en": "Zmei (masculine) and zmeya (feminine), \"serpent\", name a kind of dragon in Russian tales and epic songs, at times a human-like figure with dragonish traits. The best known are Gorynych and Tugarin, foes of the bogatyrs Dobrynya Nikitich and Alyosha Popovich. Though the word means serpent, across Russia zmei used of dragons always implies the flying kind.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "zmei-russian-folklore-c01",
    "zmei-russian-folklore-c02",
    "zmei-russian-folklore-c04"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Zmey Gorynych adalah naga cerita rakyat Rusia yang menculik perempuan. Ia biasanya tinggal di pegunungan, sering dekat sungai api, dan menjaga Jembatan Kalinov menuju alam orang mati. Kepala yang banyak adalah ciri tetapnya, biasanya kelipatan tiga seperti 3, 6, 9, atau 12; ia paling sering berkepala tiga, dan umumnya dapat terbang walau sayapnya jarang disebut.",
   "en": "Zmey Gorynych is a dragon of Russian folklore who carries off women. He usually lives in the mountains, often by a fiery river, and guards the Kalinov Bridge to the land of the dead. Many heads are his constant feature, usually a multiple of three such as 3, 6, 9 or 12; he is most often three-headed, and generally flies though his wings are seldom mentioned.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "zmei-russian-folklore-c05",
    "zmei-russian-folklore-c06"
   ]
  }
 ],

  {
   "id": "zmei-russian-folklore-c01",
   "source_id": "zmei-russian-folklore-s1",
   "quote": "meaning \"serpent\", is a form of dragon in Russian folktales and epic poetry; sometimes, a human-like character with dragon-like traits.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Zmei (laki-laki, змей) dan zmeya (perempuan), yang berarti \"ular\", adalah bentuk naga dalam dongeng dan epos Rusia, kadang tokoh mirip manusia bersifat naga.",
    "en": "Zmei (masculine) and zmeya (feminine), meaning \"serpent\", is a form of dragon in Russian folktales and epic poetry, sometimes a human-like character with dragon traits."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "zmei-russian-folklore-c02",
   "source_id": "zmei-russian-folklore-s1",
   "quote": "Two of the most famous zmei are Gorynych and Tugarin, two well-known zmei, appear as adversaries of the bogatyri (heroes) Dobrynya Nikitich or Alyosha Popovich.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dua zmei paling terkenal adalah Gorynych dan Tugarin, lawan para bogatyr Dobrynya Nikitich atau Alyosha Popovich.",
    "en": "The two most famous zmei, Gorynych and Tugarin, appear as adversaries of the bogatyrs Dobrynya Nikitich or Alyosha Popovich."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "zmei-russian-folklore-c03",
   "source_id": "zmei-russian-folklore-s1",
   "quote": "Another form is zmiy (змий, zmij), borrowed from Church Slavonic",
   "locator": "Etymology",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Bentuk lain adalah zmiy, serapan dari bahasa Slavonik Gereja.",
    "en": "Another form is zmiy, borrowed from Church Slavonic."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "zmei-russian-folklore-c04",
   "source_id": "zmei-russian-folklore-s1",
   "quote": "Despite meaning serpent, when referring to dragons, the term zmei always implicitly refers to the flying kind all over Russia.",
   "locator": "Nomenclature",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Walau berarti ular, bila merujuk naga istilah zmei di seluruh Rusia selalu menyiratkan jenis yang terbang.",
    "en": "Despite meaning serpent, when used of dragons zmei always implies the flying kind all over Russia."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "zmei-russian-folklore-c05",
   "source_id": "zmei-russian-folklore-s2",
   "quote": "Змей Горы́ныч — русский фольклорный змей (дракон), похищающий женщин. Змей Горыныч обитает обычно в горах, нередко у огненной реки и стережёт «Калинов мост», по которому попадают в царство мёртвых.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Zmey Gorynych adalah naga cerita rakyat Rusia yang menculik perempuan; ia biasanya tinggal di pegunungan, sering dekat sungai api, dan menjaga Jembatan Kalinov menuju alam orang mati.",
    "en": "Zmey Gorynych is a Russian folk dragon who abducts women; he usually lives in the mountains, often by a fiery river, guarding the Kalinov Bridge to the realm of the dead."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "zmei-russian-folklore-c06",
   "source_id": "zmei-russian-folklore-s2",
   "quote": "Многоголовость Змея — непременная его черта. Число голов обычно кратно трём, чаще всего их бывает 3, 6, 9 или 12 ... Чаще всего Змей предстаёт трёхглавым. ... В большинстве случаев у Змея отмечается способность к полёту, но о крыльях его, как правило, ничего не говорится. Так, во всём сборнике русских народных сказок Александра Афанасьева лишь один раз сообщается об «огненных крыльях»",
   "locator": "Описание",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kepala banyak adalah ciri tetap Zmey, biasanya kelipatan tiga (3, 6, 9, 12); paling sering berkepala tiga; umumnya dapat terbang tetapi sayapnya jarang disebut; dalam seluruh kumpulan dongeng Afanasyev \"sayap api\" hanya disebut sekali.",
    "en": "Many heads are the Zmey's constant trait, usually multiples of three (3, 6, 9, 12); most often three-headed; he usually flies but his wings are rarely mentioned; in Afanasyev's whole collection \"fiery wings\" appear only once."
   }
  }
 ],
 "conflicts": [],
 "gaps": [
  {
   "field": "images",
   "searched": "Lubok Zmey Gorynych di Commons belum diperiksa."
  }
 ]
}
```

```json
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Indrik adalah binatang dongeng dalam Kitab Merpati dan cerita rakyat Rusia, raja segala binatang yang membuat bumi bergetar saat bergerak.",
  "en": "Indrik is a fabulous beast of the Dove Book and Russian folklore, king of all animals, whose stirring makes the earth tremble.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "indrik-c01"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Dalam Kitab Merpati dan cerita rakyat Rusia, Indrik atau Binatang Indrik adalah raja segala binatang yang tinggal di \"Gunung Suci\" yang tak boleh diinjak kaki lain. Bila ia bergerak, bumi bergetar. Dalam legenda Rusia ia disebut \"bapak segala binatang\".",
   "en": "In the Dove Book and Russian lore, Indrik, or the Indrik-Beast, rules over every animal from a sacred peak, \"the Holy Mountain\", that no other creature may set foot on. Its slightest movement makes the earth shake. Russian legend calls it \"father of all beasts\".",
   "claim_ids": [
    "indrik-c01",
    "indrik-c04"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Ia digambarkan sebagai banteng raksasa berkaki rusa, berkepala kuda, dan bercula besar di moncongnya, sehingga agak mirip badak; dalam naskah fisiolog ia binatang bertanduk campuran berkepala dan berekor kuda dan bertubuh ikan. Nama Indrik adalah bentuk rusak dari kata Rusia untuk unicorn, yedinorog. Diduga gambaran ini mencerminkan mamut mitologis, binatang bawah tanah dalam mitologi Siberia dan Timur Jauh.",
   "en": "It is pictured as a huge bull with a deer's legs, a horse's head and a great horn on its snout, rather like a rhinoceros; physiologus texts make it a horned hybrid with a horse's head and tail and a fish's body. The name Indrik is a corrupted form of the Russian word for unicorn, yedinorog. The image may reflect the mythological mammoth, an underground beast in the myths of Siberia and the Far East.",
```

```json
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Likho adalah perwujudan nasib buruk dan kemalangan dalam mitologi Slavia, sering digambarkan sebagai perempuan tua kurus bermata satu berpakaian hitam.",
  "en": "Likho is the embodiment of evil fate and misfortune in Slavic mythology, often shown as a thin, one-eyed old woman in black.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "likho-c01"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Likho adalah perwujudan nasib buruk dan kemalangan dalam mitologi Slavia. Ia makhluk bermata satu yang sering digambarkan sebagai perempuan tua kurus berpakaian hitam, \"Likho Bermata Satu\", atau sebagai goblin hutan laki-laki yang jahat. Likho lebih banyak muncul dalam dongeng daripada dalam kepercayaan Slavia yang utama.",
   "en": "Likho personifies ill fate and bad luck in Slavic myth. It is a one-eyed being, often drawn as a gaunt old woman in black, \"One-eyed Likho\", or else as a spiteful male forest goblin. It belongs more to fairy tales than to the main body of Slavic belief.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "likho-c01",
    "likho-c02"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Ada beberapa versi kisah pertemuan manusia dengan Likho: manusia akhirnya menipunya; atau Likho menipu manusia lalu menunggang di lehernya, dan ketika orang itu mencoba menenggelamkannya di sungai, justru ia sendiri yang tenggelam sementara Likho mengapung pergi mencari korban lain. Dalam takhayul, Likho datang untuk memakan orang, dan ini dipakai untuk menakut-nakuti anak kecil. Dalam dongeng Rusia \"Likho Bermata Satu\" ia raksasa perempuan kurus bermata satu pemakan manusia.",
   "en": "Tales of meeting Likho vary: a person may finally trick it; or Likho tricks a person and rides on their neck, and when the person tries to drown it in a river they drown instead while Likho floats off to find new victims. In superstition Likho came to eat people, a threat used to scare small children. In the Russian tale \"One-eyed Likho\" it is a thin, one-eyed, man-eating giantess.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "likho-c03",
    "likho-c04",
    "likho-c06"
   ]
  }
 ],

   "accessed": "2026-10-03"
  }
 ],
 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "likho-c01",
   "source_id": "likho-s1",
   "quote": "is an embodiment of evil fate and misfortune in Slavic mythology. A creature with one eye who is often depicted as an old, skinny woman in black (Лихо одноглазое, One-eyed Likho) or as an evil male goblin of forests.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Likho (Лихо, Polandia licho) adalah perwujudan nasib buruk dan kemalangan dalam mitologi Slavia, makhluk bermata satu yang sering digambarkan sebagai perempuan tua kurus berpakaian hitam (Likho Bermata Satu) atau goblin hutan laki-laki yang jahat.",
    "en": "Likho (Polish licho) embodies evil fate and misfortune in Slavic mythology, a one-eyed creature often shown as a skinny old woman in black (One-eyed Likho) or an evil male forest goblin."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "likho-c02",
   "source_id": "likho-s1",
   "quote": "Rather than being included in the major canon of the Slavic belief system, the Likho is traditionally found in fairy tales.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Likho secara tradisional ditemukan dalam dongeng, bukan dalam kanon utama kepercayaan Slavia.",
    "en": "Likho is traditionally found in fairy tales rather than the major canon of Slavic belief."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "likho-c03",
   "source_id": "likho-s1",
   "quote": "Likho cheats a person and rides on their neck. The person wanting to drown Likho jumps into a river and drowns themself, but Likho floats out to chase other victims.",
   "locator": "Story",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Likho menipu seseorang dan menunggang di lehernya; orang yang ingin menenggelamkannya melompat ke sungai dan malah tenggelam, sementara Likho mengapung keluar untuk mengejar korban lain.",
    "en": "Likho cheats a person and rides on their neck; wanting to drown it, the person jumps into a river and drowns, while Likho floats away after other victims."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "likho-c04",
   "source_id": "likho-s1",
   "quote": "Within the framework of superstitions, Likho was supposed to come and eat a person. In particular, this was used to scare small children.",
   "locator": "Story",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam takhayul, Likho dikatakan datang dan memakan orang, dan ini dipakai untuk menakut-nakuti anak kecil; ada juga kisah manusia yang akhirnya menipu Likho.",
    "en": "In superstition Likho was supposed to come and eat a person, used especially to scare small children; there are also tales of a person finally cheating Likho."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "likho-c05",
   "source_id": "likho-s2",
   "quote": "Лихо в русском языке — «зло, несчастье», в первоначальном значении — «остаточный, лишний».",
   "locator": "Описание",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam bahasa Rusia, likho berarti \"kejahatan, kemalangan\", dengan makna asal \"sisa, berlebih\"; dalam dongeng Likho sering dikaitkan dengan Gore (Duka) dan membawa berbagai kemalangan kepada orang yang didekatinya.",
    "en": "In Russian likho means \"evil, misfortune\", originally \"leftover, superfluous\"; in tales Likho is often linked with Gore (Grief) and brings all kinds of misfortune to those it is near."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "likho-c06",
   "source_id": "likho-s2",
   "quote": "В русской народной сказке «Лихо одноглазое» предстаёт в образе худой одноглазой великанши-людоедки.",
   "locator": "Описание",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam dongeng rakyat Rusia \"Likho Bermata Satu\", Likho tampil sebagai raksasa perempuan kurus bermata satu pemakan manusia.",
    "en": "In the Russian folk tale \"One-eyed Likho\" it appears as a thin, one-eyed, man-eating giantess."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "likho-c07",
   "source_id": "likho-s1",
   "quote": "Likho is not a real proper name but a noun meaning bad luck in modern Russian and Ukrainian",
   "locator": "Nomenclature",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Likho bukan nama diri, melainkan kata benda yang berarti nasib buruk dalam bahasa Rusia dan Ukraina modern.",
    "en": "Likho is not a real proper name but a noun meaning bad luck in modern Russian and Ukrainian."
   }
  }
 ],
 "conflicts": [],
 "gaps": [
  {
   "field": "images",
   "searched": "Tidak ada gambar Commons yang terbukti menggambarkan Likho."
  }
 ]
}
```

```json
  "id": "Ördög adalah makhluk iblis pengubah wujud dalam mitologi dan paganisme awal Hungaria yang menguasai kekuatan gelap dan jahat di dunia.",
  "en": "Ördög is a shape-shifting demonic being of Hungarian mythology and early paganism who controls the dark and evil forces of the world.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "ordog-c01"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Ördög, dalam bahasa Hungaria Kuno Ürdüng, adalah makhluk iblis pengubah wujud dari mitologi dan paganisme awal Hungaria yang menguasai kekuatan gelap dan jahat di dunia. Setelah Kristenisasi ia disamakan dengan Iblis. Dalam mitologi Hungaria, Isten (Tuhan) dibantu Ördög saat menciptakan dunia.",
   "en": "Ördög, Ürdüng in Old Hungarian, is a form-changing demon of Hungarian myth and early paganism who commands the world's dark and evil powers. After Christianisation he was equated with the Devil. In Hungarian myth Isten, God, had Ördög's help in creating the world.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "ordog-c01",
    "ordog-c02"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Ia agak mirip satir atau faun: tubuh atas lelaki dan tubuh bawah kambing, biasanya hitam pekat, berkuku belah, bertanduk domba, berekor panjang yang ujungnya seperti bilah, dan membawa garpu. Ia tinggal di dunia bawah atau neraka, Pokol, sambil terus mengaduk kuali besar berisi jiwa orang berdosa. Bila datang ke bumi ia bersembunyi di dinding rumah korbannya, atau menjelma rubah, nyala api gelap, atau gembala Hungaria bermata gelap berkilat; ia suka bertaruh dengan manusia untuk mengumpulkan jiwa.",
   "en": "He resembles a satyr or faun, man above and goat below, usually jet black, with cloven hooves, ram's horns, a long blade-tipped tail and a pitchfork. He lives in the underworld or hell, Pokol, forever stirring a vast cauldron of sinners' souls. On earth he may lurk inside his victims' walls making faint squeaks, or take the guise of a fox, a dark flame or a Hungarian herdsman whose dark eyes sparkle; he likes to wager with people in order to win their souls.",
   "claim_ids": [

   "id": "ordog-c01",
   "source_id": "ordog-s1",
   "quote": "(Ürdüng in Old Hungarian) is a shape-shifting, demonic creature from Hungarian mythology and early Hungarian paganism who controls the dark and evil forces of the world.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ördög (Ürdüng dalam bahasa Hungaria Kuno) adalah makhluk iblis pengubah wujud dari mitologi dan paganisme awal Hungaria yang menguasai kekuatan gelap dan jahat di dunia.",
    "en": "Ördög (Ürdüng in Old Hungarian) is a shape-shifting demonic creature of Hungarian mythology and early paganism who controls the dark and evil forces of the world."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ordog-c02",
   "source_id": "ordog-s1",
   "quote": "After Christianization, it was identified with the devil. In Hungarian mythology God (Isten in Hungarian) had help from Ördög when creating the world.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Setelah Kristenisasi ia disamakan dengan Iblis; dalam mitologi Hungaria Tuhan (Isten) dibantu Ördög saat menciptakan dunia.",
    "en": "After Christianisation it was identified with the Devil; in Hungarian mythology God (Isten) had Ördög's help in creating the world."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ordog-c03",
   "source_id": "ordog-s1",
   "quote": "Ördög looks somewhat like a satyr or faun, a humanoid with the upper torso of a human male and lower portions of a goat; usually pitch-black, with cloven hooves, ram-like horns, a long tail ending in a blade; and he carries a pitchfork.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ördög agak mirip satir atau faun, bertubuh atas lelaki dan bawah kambing, biasanya hitam pekat, berkuku belah, bertanduk domba, berekor panjang berujung bilah, dan membawa garpu.",
    "en": "Ördög looks somewhat like a satyr or faun, man above and goat below, usually pitch-black, with cloven hooves, ram-like horns, a long blade-tipped tail, carrying a pitchfork."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ordog-c04",
   "source_id": "ordog-s1",
   "quote": "He dwells in the underworld or hell (Pokol in Hungarian), constantly stirring a huge cauldron filled with souls of those who lived in sin (however, it is uncertain whether the underworld was regarded as place of punishment or not in pre-Christian Hungarian mythology",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia tinggal di dunia bawah atau neraka (Pokol), terus mengaduk kuali besar berisi jiwa orang berdosa; namun tidak pasti apakah dunia bawah dianggap tempat hukuman dalam mitologi Hungaria pra-Kristen.",
    "en": "He dwells in the underworld or hell (Pokol), constantly stirring a huge cauldron of sinners' souls; it is uncertain, however, whether the underworld was a place of punishment in pre-Christian Hungarian mythology."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ordog-c05",
   "source_id": "ordog-s1",
   "quote": "In other legends, when he comes to earth, he takes the form of a fox, a dark flame or a Hungarian shepherd with dark, sparkling eyes. It is his habit to make bets with humans to see if they become corrupted. His long-term goal is to collect more human souls",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam legenda lain, saat ke bumi ia menjelma rubah, nyala api gelap, atau gembala Hungaria bermata gelap berkilat; ia biasa bertaruh dengan manusia untuk melihat apakah mereka menjadi rusak, dengan tujuan mengumpulkan jiwa; menurut legenda lain ia bersembunyi di dinding rumah korban.",
    "en": "In other legends, on earth he becomes a fox, a dark flame or a Hungarian shepherd with dark, sparkling eyes; he habitually bets with humans to see if they become corrupted, aiming to collect souls; other legends have him hide in victims' walls."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ordog-c06",
   "source_id": "ordog-s2",
   "quote": "Когда он всё-таки приходит на землю, то, согласно некоторым легендам, он прячется в стенах жертв и издаёт едва уловимые звуки, звучащие пронзительно и даже пискляво.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut beberapa legenda, saat datang ke bumi ia bersembunyi di dinding rumah korban dan mengeluarkan bunyi halus yang melengking.",
    "en": "According to some legends, when he comes to earth he hides in victims' walls and makes faint, shrill, squeaky sounds."
   }
  }
 ],
```

```json
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Vedmak adalah dukun atau penyihir laki-laki dalam mitologi Slavia, pasangan laki-laki dari vedma (penyihir perempuan), yang bisa menyembuhkan maupun mencelakai.",
  "en": "A vedmak is a warlock or male witch of Slavic mythology, the male counterpart of the vedma, who can both heal and harm.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "vedmak-c01"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Dalam mitologi Slavia, vedmak adalah dukun atau penyihir laki-laki; padanan perempuannya adalah vedma. Peran ini sangat menekankan sisi syamanistik paganisme Slavia: mereka mengobati manusia dan hewan, tetapi juga dianggap terhubung dengan iblis dan mampu mencelakai dengan mengirim penyakit, membunuh ternak, atau merusak panen. Dalam demonologi Slavia ia juga pemimpin para penyihir di suatu daerah, yang bisa bekerja sama dengan mereka atau justru melindungi orang dari ulah mereka.",
   "en": "Slavic myth calls a warlock or male witch a vedmak; the woman's counterpart is the vedma. The role leans heavily on the shamanic side of Slavic paganism: such men heal people and animals, yet are also thought to be in league with the devil and able to do harm by sending sickness, killing livestock or ruining harvests. In Slavic demonology he is also the head of the witches of a district, either working with them or shielding people from their mischief.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "vedmak-c01",
    "vedmak-c02"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Vedmak dapat berubah menjadi binatang atau benda apa pun, misalnya ngengat, kuda, atau serigala. Menurut kepercayaan ia bisa \"terlahir\" atau \"terlatih\"; vedmak yang terlahir mungkin tak berjanggut tetapi berekor kecil. Kata vedmak berasal dari bahasa Proto-Slavia *vědět, \"mengetahui\". Karena saga fantasi The Witcher karya Andrzej Sapkowski, istilah ini kadang diterjemahkan sebagai \"witcher\" dalam bahasa Inggris.",
   "en": "A vedmak can turn into any animal or object, such as a moth, a horse or a wolf. Belief holds that he may be \"born\" or \"taught\"; a born vedmak may lack a beard but have a small tail. The word comes from Proto-Slavic *vědět, \"to know\". Because of Andrzej Sapkowski's fantasy saga The Witcher, the term is sometimes rendered in English as \"witcher\".",
   "claim_ids": [
    "vedmak-c03",
    "vedmak-c04",
    "vedmak-c05",
    "vedmak-c06",
    "vedmak-c07"
   ]
  }
 ],

   "title": "Ведьмак (мифология)",
   "author": null,

   "published": null,
   "language": "ru",

 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "vedmak-c01",
   "source_id": "vedmak-s1",
   "quote": "is a warlock or male witch, the female equivalent (witch) being vedma. This role greatly focuses on the Shamanic aspects of Slavic paganism.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam mitologi Slavia, vedmak adalah dukun atau penyihir laki-laki, padanan perempuannya vedma; peran ini menekankan sisi syamanistik paganisme Slavia: mengobati manusia dan hewan, tetapi juga dianggap terhubung dengan iblis dan mampu mencelakai dengan penyakit, membunuh ternak, atau merusak panen.",
    "en": "In Slavic mythology a vedmak is a warlock or male witch, the female equivalent being the vedma; the role stresses the shamanic side of Slavic paganism: treating people and animals, yet thought linked to the devil and able to harm by sickness, killing cattle or spoiling harvests."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vedmak-c02",
   "source_id": "vedmak-s2",
   "quote": "персонаж славянской мифологии и демонологии, колдун. Является начальником над ведьмами определённой местности; может действовать заодно с ними или, наоборот, защищать людей от проделок ведьм и заложных покойников, заговорами лечить болезни людей и животных.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Vedmak (Ведьмак, Polandia wiedźmak) adalah tokoh mitologi dan demonologi Slavia, seorang penyihir, pemimpin para penyihir di suatu daerah, yang bisa bekerja sama dengan mereka atau melindungi orang dari ulah mereka dan dari arwah penasaran, serta menyembuhkan penyakit manusia dan hewan dengan mantra.",
    "en": "The vedmak is a figure of Slavic mythology and demonology, a sorcerer heading the witches of a district, who may side with them or protect people from witches and restless dead, healing people and animals with charms."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vedmak-c03",
   "source_id": "vedmak-s1",
   "quote": "A vedmak can turn into any animal or any object.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Vedmak dapat berubah menjadi binatang atau benda apa pun.",
    "en": "A vedmak can turn into any animal or object."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vedmak-c04",
   "source_id": "vedmak-s2",
   "quote": "По поверьям, ведьмак, как и другие колдуны, может быть «прирождённым» или «наученным». У «прирождённого» ведьмака может не быть бороды, усов, зато присутствует маленький хвостик",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut kepercayaan, vedmak seperti penyihir lain bisa \"terlahir\" atau \"terlatih\"; vedmak yang terlahir mungkin tak berjanggut dan berkumis, tetapi memiliki ekor kecil.",
    "en": "By belief a vedmak, like other sorcerers, may be \"born\" or \"taught\"; a born vedmak may lack beard and moustache but have a small tail."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vedmak-c05",
   "source_id": "vedmak-s1",
   "quote": "Vedmak stems from Proto-Slavic *vědět (\"to know\") and Old East Slavic вѣдь (\"knowledge; witchcraft\"",
   "locator": "Etymology",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kata vedmak berasal dari Proto-Slavia *vědět (\"mengetahui\") dan Slavia Timur Kuno вѣдь (\"pengetahuan; ilmu sihir\").",
    "en": "Vedmak stems from Proto-Slavic *vědět (\"to know\") and Old East Slavic вѣдь (\"knowledge; witchcraft\")."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vedmak-c06",
   "source_id": "vedmak-s1",
   "quote": "Under the influence of The Witcher fantasy saga by Andrzej Sapkowski, the term vedmak is sometimes also rendered as \"witcher\" in English in certain contexts.",
   "locator": "The Witcher",
   "context": "modern-popular-culture",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Karena pengaruh saga fantasi The Witcher karya Andrzej Sapkowski, istilah vedmak kadang diterjemahkan sebagai \"witcher\" dalam bahasa Inggris.",
    "en": "Under the influence of Andrzej Sapkowski's fantasy saga The Witcher, vedmak is sometimes rendered as \"witcher\" in English."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vedmak-c07",
   "source_id": "vedmak-s2",
   "quote": "Обладая магическими способностями, ведьмак может по своему усмотрению оборачиваться мотыльком, конём, волком или другими животными.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dengan kemampuan sihirnya, vedmak dapat sesuka hati berubah menjadi ngengat, kuda, serigala, atau binatang lain.",
    "en": "With his magic powers a vedmak can turn at will into a moth, horse, wolf or other animals."
   }
  }
 ],
```

```json
  "id": "Berehynia adalah roh rendah dalam mitologi Slavia Timur yang berkerabat dengan rusalka; gambaran modernnya sebagai dewi Ukraina kuno dianggap tidak berdasar oleh para peneliti.",
  "en": "Berehynia is a lesser spirit of East Slavic mythology related to the rusalki; her modern image as an ancient Ukrainian goddess is considered baseless by scholars.",

 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Berehynia, mungkin sama dengan perehenia, adalah makhluk dalam mitologi Slavia Timur, roh rendah yang berkerabat dengan rusalka. Namanya dikaitkan dengan tepi sungai (bereh) dan bukit. Dalam sedikit sumber kuno yang menyebutnya, berehynia selalu muncul dalam bentuk jamak, dan wujud serta fungsinya tidak diketahui. Sebagian peneliti menganggapnya dekat dengan rusalka, yaitu gadis yang mati tenggelam atau meninggal sebelum dibaptis.",
   "en": "Berehynia, perhaps the same as perehenia, is a being of East Slavic myth, a lesser spirit akin to the rusalki. The name has been tied to riverbanks (bereh) and hills. In the few fragmentary old mentions, berehyni appear only in the plural, and their look and exact role are unknown. Some scholars placed them close to rusalki, drowned girls or girls who died unbaptised.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "berehynia-c01",
    "berehynia-c03",
    "berehynia-c06"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Sejak sekitar tahun 1980-an berehynia ditafsirkan sebagai roh perempuan penjaga rumah dan keluarga. Gambaran populer modern tentang Berehynia sebagai dewi pagan Ukraina kuno tidak punya dasar sejarah, etnografi, maupun arkeologi; kebanyakan akademisi menganggapnya produk pseudoilmiah dari karya sastra romantis. Pada akhir abad ke-20, nasionalisme romantis Ukraina memusatkan perhatian pada mitos matriarkal ini.",
   "en": "Since about the 1980s berehyni have been read as female spirits guarding home and family. The popular modern image of Berehynia as an ancient Ukrainian pagan goddess has no historical, ethnographic or archaeological basis; most scholars see it as a pseudoscientific product of romantic literature.  "short_description": {
```
```json
  "id": "Naga Brosno atau Brosnya adalah monster danau dalam cerita rakyat Rusia yang konon menghuni Danau Brosno dekat Andreapol, Rusia barat.",
  "en": "The Brosno dragon or Brosnya is a lake monster of Russian folklore said to inhabit Lake Brosno near Andreapol in western Russia.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "brosno-dragon-c01"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Naga Brosno, juga dikenal sebagai Brosnya, adalah monster danau yang menurut cerita rakyat Rusia menghuni Danau Brosno dekat Andreapol di Rusia barat. Ia digambarkan mirip naga dan menjadi pokok sejumlah legenda daerah, sebagian disebut berasal dari abad ke-13.",
   "en": "The Brosno dragon, also called Brosnya, is a lake monster that Russian folk tradition places in Lake Brosno near Andreapol in western Russia. It is described as dragon-like and features in several local legends, some reportedly reaching back to the 13th century.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "brosno-dragon-c01"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Pada abad ke-18 dan ke-19 beredar kabar bahwa makhluk raksasa itu muncul ke permukaan danau pada senja hari tetapi segera menyelam bila orang mendekat. Konon pada Perang Dunia II ia menelan sebuah pesawat Jerman. Banyak orang meragukan keberadaannya dan menduga makhluk itu berang-berang atau ikan tombak raksasa. Penduduk setempat masih mengaku melihat kadal besar atau mendengar \"ia bernapas di bawah air\"; kedalaman danau, letaknya yang terpencil, dan gelembung gas dari pembusukan di dasar danau mungkin menjelaskan kisah-kisah itu.",
   "en": "In the 18th and 19th centuries rumour held that the giant creature surfaced on the lake in the evening but dived at once when people came near. It is said to have swallowed a German aeroplane in World War II. Many are sceptical and suggest it may be a beaver or a giant pike. Locals still report seeing a big lizard or hearing \"it breathe under water\"; the lake's great depth, its remoteness and gas bubbles from decay at the bottom may explain the tales.",

 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "brosno-dragon-c01",
   "source_id": "brosno-dragon-s1",
   "quote": "is a supposed lake monster which in Russian folklore is said to inhabit Lake Brosno near Andreapol in western Russia. It is described as resembling a dragon and is the subject of a number of regional legends, some which are said to date back to the 13th century.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Naga Brosno (Brosnya, Бросня) adalah monster danau yang menurut cerita rakyat Rusia menghuni Danau Brosno dekat Andreapol di Rusia barat, digambarkan mirip naga, dan menjadi pokok sejumlah legenda daerah yang sebagian disebut berasal dari abad ke-13.",
    "en": "The Brosno dragon (Brosnya) is a supposed lake monster said in Russian folklore to inhabit Lake Brosno near Andreapol in western Russia, described as dragon-like and the subject of regional legends, some said to date to the 13th century."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "brosno-dragon-c02",
   "source_id": "brosno-dragon-s1",
   "quote": "It was rumored in the 18th and 19th centuries that the giant creature emerged to the lake's surface in the evening, but immediately submerged when people approached.",
   "locator": "History",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Pada abad ke-18 dan ke-19 beredar kabar bahwa makhluk raksasa itu muncul ke permukaan danau pada senja hari tetapi segera menyelam bila orang mendekat.",
    "en": "In the 18th and 19th centuries it was rumoured that the giant creature surfaced in the evening but dived at once when people approached."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "brosno-dragon-c03",
   "source_id": "brosno-dragon-s1",
   "quote": "It is said that during World War II the beast swallowed up a German airplane.",
   "locator": "History",
   "context": "modern-popular-culture",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Konon pada Perang Dunia II binatang itu menelan sebuah pesawat Jerman.",
    "en": "It is said that during World War II the beast swallowed a German aeroplane."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "brosno-dragon-c04",
   "source_id": "brosno-dragon-s1",
   "quote": "Many people treat the existence of Brosnya skeptically and say that the creature may be a beaver or a giant pike.",
   "locator": "Theories",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Banyak orang meragukan keberadaan Brosnya dan menduga makhluk itu berang-berang atau ikan tombak raksasa.",
    "en": "Many are sceptical about Brosnya and suggest it may be a beaver or a giant pike."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "brosno-dragon-c05",
   "source_id": "brosno-dragon-s2",
   "quote": "Согласно легендам и многочисленным заявлениям местных жителей, озеро Бросно — место обитания «дракона», мифического животного. Местные жители периодически говорят, что видели большого ящера или слышали, «как он дышит под водой».",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut legenda dan banyak pernyataan penduduk setempat, Danau Brosno adalah tempat tinggal \"naga\", binatang mitis; penduduk kadang mengaku melihat kadal besar atau mendengar \"ia bernapas di bawah air\".",
    "en": "According to legends and many local accounts, Lake Brosno is the home of a \"dragon\", a mythical animal; locals periodically say they saw a large lizard or heard \"it breathing under water\"."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "brosno-dragon-c06",
   "source_id": "brosno-dragon-s2",
   "quote": "Причиной возникновения подобных легенд, скорее всего, стала необычно большая глубина озера и удалённость его от крупных населённых пунктов. Кроме того, процессы гниения, происходящие на глубине, приводят к образованию сероводородных «пузырей», выходящих на поверхность.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Legenda ini kemungkinan besar muncul karena danau itu sangat dalam dan jauh dari permukiman besar; selain itu pembusukan di dasar danau menghasilkan gelembung hidrogen sulfida yang naik ke permukaan.",
    "en": "Such legends most likely arose from the lake's unusual depth and remoteness from large settlements; in addition, decay at depth produces hydrogen-sulphide \"bubbles\" rising to the surface."
   }
  }
 ],
```

```json
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Kulshedra atau kuçedra adalah iblis air, badai, api, dan dunia bawah dalam mitologi Albania, biasanya naga ular betina raksasa berkepala banyak.",
  "en": "Albanian lore pictures the kulshedra (kuçedra) as a giant she-dragon of many serpent heads, a demon of water, storms, fire and the depths of the earth.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "kulshedra-c01"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Dalam mitologi dan cerita rakyat Albania, kulshedra atau kuçedra adalah iblis air, badai, api, dan dunia bawah, biasanya digambarkan sebagai naga ular betina raksasa berkepala banyak. Ia arketipe kegelapan dan kejahatan, lawan sekaligus pelengkap drangue. Ia dipercaya menyemburkan api serta menimbulkan kekeringan, badai, banjir, dan gempa bumi.",
   "en": "Albanian myth and folklore describe the kulshedra or kuçedra as a demon of water, storm, fire and the underworld, usually a vast many-headed she-dragon in serpent form. She embodies darkness and evil, the counterpart and foe of the drangue. She is thought to breathe fire and bring drought, storms, floods and earthquakes.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "kulshedra-c01"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Ia biasanya dilawan dan dikalahkan drangue, dan badai petir besar dianggap akibat pertempuran mereka. Tahap awalnya adalah bolla, ular iblis yang matanya tertutup sepanjang tahun kecuali pada Hari Santo Georgius; bila bolla hidup bertahun-tahun tanpa terlihat manusia, ia berubah menjadi kulshedra. Kulshedra juga bisa menyamar sebagai perempuan.",
   "en": "She is usually fought and beaten by the drangue, and heavy thunderstorms are thought to come from their battles. Her early stage is the bolla, a demonic serpent whose eyes stay shut all year except on Saint George's Day; if the bolla lives the set number of years unseen by humans, it becomes a kulshedra. A kulshedra can also go about disguised as a woman.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "kulshedra-c02",
    "kulshedra-c04",
    "kulshedra-c05"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Ia digambarkan sebagai naga betina berwujud ular berkepala banyak, dengan payudara menjuntai sampai ke tanah, tubuh berbulu merah, ekor panjang, dan tujuh sampai dua belas kepala. Namanya berasal dari bahasa Latin chersydrus, dari bahasa Yunani, yang kira-kira berarti \"ular amfibi\". Dalam dongeng ia menguasai mata air dan memakan orang yang mendekatinya. Desa tempat kulshedra tinggal dikatakan makmur karena napasnya menyerap bahan makanan dari mana-mana.",
   "en": "She is pictured as a many-headed serpentine she-dragon with drooping breasts that touch the ground, red woolly hair, a long tail and seven to twelve heads. Her name comes from Latin chersydrus, from Greek, roughly \"amphibious snake\". In folk tales she seizes water springs and eats anyone who comes near them. The village where a kulshedra lives is said to prosper, as her breath draws in foodstuffs from everywhere.",
   "claim_ids": [

   ]

    "en": "The kulshedra or kuçedra is a water, storm, fire and chthonic demon of Albanian myth, usually a huge many-headed female serpent-dragon; archetype of darkness and evil opposing the drangue; believed to spit fire and cause drought, storms, floods, earthquakes and other disasters."

  {
   "id": "kulshedra-c02",
   "source_id": "kulshedra-s1",
   "quote": "In Albanian mythology she is usually fought and defeated by a drangue, who is a semi-human winged divine hero and protector of mankind, the most widespread Albanian culture hero. Heavy thunderstorms are thought to be the result of most of their battles.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam mitologi Albania ia biasanya dilawan dan dikalahkan drangue, pahlawan ilahi bersayap pelindung manusia; badai petir besar dianggap akibat pertempuran mereka; ia juga dilawan pahlawan langit lain seperti E Bija e Hënës dhe e Diellit.",
    "en": "In Albanian mythology she is usually fought and defeated by a drangue, a winged divine protector of mankind; heavy thunderstorms are thought to result from their battles; other sky heroes such as E Bija e Hënës dhe e Diellit also fight her."
   }

   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kuçedër (kuçedra) berasal dari bahasa Latin chersydrus (dari bahasa Yunani), yang kira-kira berarti \"ular amfibi\".",
    "en": "Kuçedër (kuçedra) derives from Latin chersydrus (from Greek), roughly \"amphibious snake\"."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "kulshedra-c04",
   "source_id": "kulshedra-s1",
   "quote": "According to folk beliefs, the kulshedra's earlier stage is the bolla, which takes the form of demonic serpent possessing both chthonic and aquatic attributes. The bolla's eyes remain shut for the whole year except on Saint George's Day ... The bolla will eventually develop into a kulshedra if it should manage to live for the prescribed number of years without being seen by a human.",
   "locator": "Names",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut kepercayaan rakyat, tahap awal kulshedra adalah bolla, ular iblis bersifat dunia bawah dan air yang matanya tertutup sepanjang tahun kecuali pada Hari Santo Georgius; bila hidup bertahun-tahun tanpa terlihat manusia, bolla berubah menjadi kulshedra.",
    "en": "In folk belief the kulshedra's earlier stage is the bolla, a demonic chthonic-aquatic serpent whose eyes stay shut all year except on Saint George's Day; if it lives the set years unseen by humans it becomes a kulshedra."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "kulshedra-c05",
   "source_id": "kulshedra-s1",
   "quote": "The kulshedra can also appear disguised as a woman, who keeps her true nature hidden",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kulshedra juga bisa muncul menyamar sebagai perempuan yang menyembunyikan sifat aslinya.",
    "en": "The kulshedra can also appear disguised as a woman hiding her true nature."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "kulshedra-c06",
   "source_id": "kulshedra-s1",
   "quote": "Kulshedra is generally considered to be a female dragon, like a multi-headed serpent form, but it is known to have pendulous drooping breasts touching the ground",
   "locator": "Kulshedra",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kulshedra umumnya dianggap naga betina berwujud ular berkepala banyak dengan payudara menjuntai sampai ke tanah, berbulu merah, berekor panjang, dan berkepala tujuh sampai dua belas.",
    "en": "The kulshedra is generally a female dragon like a many-headed serpent with drooping breasts touching the ground, red woolly hair, a long tail and seven to twelve heads."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "kulshedra-c07",
   "source_id": "kulshedra-s1",
   "quote": "It is said that the village where the kulshedra lives has great prosperity in agriculture and livestock. Indeed, she absorbs by her breath foodstuffs from everywhere, and her village thrives, while the affected villages become poor and do not prosper.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Desa tempat kulshedra tinggal dikatakan makmur dalam pertanian dan ternak, karena napasnya menyerap bahan makanan dari mana-mana, sementara desa lain menjadi miskin.",
    "en": "The village where the kulshedra lives is said to prosper in farming and livestock, as her breath absorbs foodstuffs from everywhere, while other villages grow poor."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "kulshedra-c08",
   "source_id": "kulshedra-s2",
   "quote": "Kuçedra ose kulshedra është një figurë mitologjike e përrallave dhe miteve popullore shqiptare që përfytyrohej herë si një gjarpër i madh me 7 ose 12 koka, apo një dragua me shumë koka e me këmbë që zakonisht zinte burimet e ujit dhe hante njerëzit që i afroheshin burimeve.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kuçedra atau kulshedra adalah tokoh mitologis dongeng dan mitos rakyat Albania yang dibayangkan sebagai ular besar berkepala 7 atau 12 (tujuh, dua belas), atau naga berkepala banyak berkaki yang biasanya menguasai mata air dan memakan orang yang mendekat.",
    "en": "Kuçedra or kulshedra is a mythological figure of Albanian folk tales and myths imagined as a great snake with 7 or 12 heads, or a many-headed legged dragon who usually seized water springs and ate people approaching them."
   }
  }
 ],
```

```json
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Potrimpo adalah dewa laut, bumi, gandum, dan tanaman dalam mitologi pagan Baltik dan Prusia, salah satu dari tiga dewa utama Prusia Kuno.",
  "en": "Potrimpo was a god of seas, earth, grain and crops in pagan Baltic and Prussian mythology, one of the three main gods of the Old Prussians.",
  "claim_ids": [

  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Potrimpo, juga Potrimpus, Autrimpo, atau Natrimpe, adalah dewa laut, bumi, gandum, dan tanaman dalam mitologi pagan Baltik dan Prusia. Ia salah satu dari tiga dewa utama yang dipuja orang Prusia Kuno, meski sebagian besar pengetahuan tentangnya berasal dari sumber abad ke-16 yang kurang dapat diandalkan.",
   "en": "Potrimpo, also Potrimpus, Autrimpo or Natrimpe, was a god of seas, earth, grain and crops in pagan Baltic and Prussian myth. He was one of the three chief gods of the Old Prussians, though most of what is known comes from unreliable 16th-century sources.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "potrimpo-c01"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Ia pertama kali disebut, sebagai Natrimpe, dalam memorandum tahun 1418 dari Uskup Warmia kepada Paus Martinus V. Simon Grunau menyebutnya dewa gandum yang bersama dewa guntur Perkūnas dan dewa kematian Peckols membentuk tritunggal pagan; ia digambarkan sebagai pemuda riang berkalung untaian bulir gandum, dan ular sebagai makhluknya dipuja serta diberi susu. Dalam mitologi Baltik ia juga disebut dewa air dan dewa sihir berwujud ular, penguasa kehidupan, kesuburan, dan kematian.",
   "en": "He is first named, as Natrimpe, in a 1418 memorandum from the Bishop of Warmia to Pope Martin V. Simon Grunau called him a grain god forming a pagan trinity with the thunder god Perkūnas and the death god Peckols; he was shown as a merry youth crowned with ears of grain, and snakes, his creatures, were worshipped and given milk. Baltic myth also makes him a water god and god of magic in serpent form, ruler of life, fertility and death.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "potrimpo-c02",
```

```json
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Strzyga biasanya adalah iblis perempuan mirip vampir dalam mitologi Slavia, terutama dalam cerita rakyat Polandia dan Silesia, yang berasal dari Strix Romawi dan Yunani kuno.",
  "en": "The strzyga is usually a female vampire-like demon of Slavic mythology, chiefly in Polish and Silesian folklore, stemming from the Strix of ancient Rome and Greece.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "strzyga-c01"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Strzyga (jamak strzygi, laki-laki strzygoń) biasanya iblis perempuan dalam mitologi Slavia yang berasal dari Strix dalam mitologi Romawi dan Yunani kuno. Ia mirip vampir dan terutama ditemukan dalam cerita rakyat Polandia dan Silesia. Menurut Aleksander Brückner, kata ini berasal dari strix, burung hantu dalam bahasa Latin, makhluk mirip burung yang memakan daging dan darah manusia.",
   "en": "The strzyga (plural strzygi, male strzygoń) is usually a female demon of Slavic myth derived from the Strix of Roman and Greek antiquity. Vampire-like, she belongs mostly to Polish and Silesian folklore. Aleksander Brückner traced the word to strix, Latin for an owl, a bird-like being that fed on human flesh and blood.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "strzyga-c01",
    "strzyga-c02"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Menurut kepercayaan Silesia, strzyga lahir sebagai strzyga: semasa hidup ia punya dua hati, dua jiwa, dan dua baris gigi. Setelah ia mati, satu jiwa meninggalkan tubuh, sementara jiwa lain tetap hidup di dalam jasad di peti. Agar ia tidak mencelakai orang, makamnya digali dan jantungnya ditusuk pasak kayu ek, atau kepalanya dipenggal; orang yang dicurigai akan menjadi strzyga dikuburkan telungkup dengan batu di bawah bibirnya. Strzyga kadang juga digambarkan sebagai burung hantu yang tinggal di hutan gelap.",
   "en": "Silesian belief held that a strzyga is born one: in life she has two hearts, two souls and two rows of teeth. When she dies one soul leaves while the other lives on in the body in the coffin. To stop her harming people, the grave was opened and her heart pierced with an oak stake, or her head cut off; someone suspected of becoming a strzyga was buried face down with a stone under the lips. Strzygi were also sometimes pictured as owls living in dark forests.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "strzyga-c05",
    "strzyga-c06",
    "strzyga-c07"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Strzyga tetap populer dalam cerita rakyat pedesaan Polandia hingga akhir abad ke-19 dan awal abad ke-20, seperti tampak dalam novel Chłopi karya Władysław Reymont. Setelah abad ke-18 dibedakan antara strzyga, yang lebih terkait dengan sihir, dan upiór, makhluk vampir yang terbang.",
   "en": "The strzyga stayed popular in rural Polish folklore into the late 19th and early 20th century, as Władysław Reymont's novel Chłopi shows. After the 18th century a line was drawn between the strzyga, tied more to witchcraft, and the upiór, a flying vampire.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "strzyga-c03",
    "strzyga-c04"
   ]
  }
 ],

 ],
 "images": [],
 "sources": [
  {
   "id": "strzyga-s1",
   "url": "https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strzyga",

   "publisher": "Wikipedia (en)",
   "published": null,
   "language": "en",
   "type": "wikipedia",
   "accessed": "2026-10-03"
  },
  {
   "id": "strzyga-s2",
   "url": "https://ru.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D0%A1%D1%82%D1%80%D1%8B%D0%B3%D0%B0",
   "title": "Стрыга",
   "author": null,
   "publisher": "Википедия (ru)",
   "published": null,
   "language": "ru",
   "type": "wikipedia",
   "accessed": "2026-10-03"
  }
 ],
 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "strzyga-c01",
   "source_id": "strzyga-s1",
   "quote": "is usually a female demon in Slavic mythology, which stems from the mythological Strix of ancient Rome and ancient Greece. The demon is similar to a vampire, and is predominantly found in Polish and Silesian folklore.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Strzyga (jamak strzygi, laki-laki strzygoń; kadang diterjemahkan striga) biasanya iblis perempuan dalam mitologi Slavia yang berasal dari Strix Romawi dan Yunani kuno, mirip vampir, dan terutama ditemukan dalam cerita rakyat Polandia dan Silesia.",
    "en": "The strzyga (plural strzygi, male strzygoń; sometimes striga) is usually a female demon of Slavic mythology stemming from the Strix of ancient Rome and Greece, vampire-like, found mainly in Polish and Silesian folklore."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "strzyga-c02",
   "source_id": "strzyga-s1",
   "quote": "According to Aleksander Brückner, the word is derived from Strix, Latin for owl and a bird-like creature which fed on human flesh and blood in Roman and Greek mythology. Hungarian sztriga, the Albanian shtriga and Romanian: strigă are also cognate and related.",
   "locator": "Etymology and origin",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut Aleksander Brückner, kata ini berasal dari strix, bahasa Latin untuk burung hantu dan makhluk mirip burung pemakan daging dan darah manusia dalam mitologi Romawi dan Yunani; kata Hungaria sztriga, Albania shtriga, dan Rumania strigă berkerabat.",
    "en": "According to Aleksander Brückner the word derives from strix, Latin for owl and a bird-like creature feeding on human flesh and blood in Roman and Greek myth; Hungarian sztriga, Albanian shtriga and Romanian strigă are cognate."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "strzyga-c03",
   "source_id": "strzyga-s1",
   "quote": "The strzyga remained a popular element in the folklore of rural Poland well into the late 19th and early 20th century, as shown by Władysław Reymont in his Nobel Prize-winning novel Chłopi (The Peasants).",
   "locator": "Etymology and origin",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Strzyga tetap populer dalam cerita rakyat pedesaan Polandia hingga akhir abad ke-19 dan awal abad ke-20, seperti ditunjukkan Władysław Reymont dalam novel Chłopi (Para Petani) yang meraih Hadiah Nobel.",
    "en": "The strzyga remained popular in rural Polish folklore into the late 19th and early 20th century, as Władysław Reymont showed in his Nobel Prize-winning novel Chłopi (The Peasants)."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "strzyga-c04",
   "source_id": "strzyga-s1",
   "quote": "After the 18th century, there was a distinction between strzyga and upiór; the first one was more connected to witchcraft, while the latter was more of a flying, vampiric creature.",
   "locator": "Etymology and origin",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Setelah abad ke-18 dibedakan antara strzyga dan upiór: yang pertama lebih terkait sihir, yang kedua lebih berupa makhluk vampir yang terbang.",
    "en": "After the 18th century strzyga and upiór were distinguished: the first tied more to witchcraft, the second more a flying vampiric creature."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "strzyga-c05",
   "source_id": "strzyga-s2",
   "quote": "Чтобы стрыга не могла вредить людям, нужно было выкопать её и проколоть ей сердце дубовым колышком ... либо отрезать ей голову и затем ударить её по ногам. Если кого-то при жизни могли заподозрить, что после смерти он станет стрыгой, то его хоронили ртом вниз и вставляли ему под губы камень",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Agar strzyga (Стрыга) tidak mencelakai orang, makamnya digali dan jantungnya ditusuk pasak kayu ek, atau kepalanya dipenggal; orang yang dicurigai akan menjadi strzyga dikuburkan telungkup dengan batu di bawah bibirnya.",
    "en": "To stop a strzyga harming people she was dug up and her heart pierced with an oak stake, or her head cut off; someone suspected of becoming one was buried face down with a stone under the lips."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "strzyga-c06",
   "source_id": "strzyga-s2",
   "quote": "В отличие от вампиров, стрыгоем, согласно силезским поверьям, человек рождался сразу. При жизни стрыга имеет два сердца, две души и два ряда зубов ... Известны случаи, когда за стрыг принимали младенцев, рождавшихся с развитыми зубами. Считалось, что после смерти стрыги одна душа покидала тело, а другая оставалась в нём и продолжала жить в теле в гробу",
   "locator": "Внутреннее строение и образ жизни",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut kepercayaan Silesia, strzyga lahir sebagai strzyga; semasa hidup ia punya dua hati, dua jiwa, dan dua baris gigi; bayi yang lahir dengan gigi kadang disangka strzyga; setelah mati satu jiwa pergi dan yang lain tetap hidup di dalam jasad di peti.",
    "en": "In Silesian belief a person was born a strzyga; in life she has two hearts, two souls and two rows of teeth; babies born with developed teeth were sometimes taken for strzygi; after death one soul leaves and the other lives on in the body in the coffin."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "strzyga-c07",
   "source_id": "strzyga-s2",
   "quote": "Стрыги также изображались иногда и как совы, обитающие в тёмных лесах",
   "locator": "Внутреннее строение и образ жизни",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Strzyga kadang juga digambarkan sebagai burung hantu yang tinggal di hutan gelap.",
    "en": "Strzygi were also sometimes depicted as owls living in dark forests."
   }
  }
 ],
 "conflicts": [],
 "gaps": [
  {
   "field": "images",
   "searched": "Tidak ada gambar Commons yang terbukti menggambarkan strzyga."
  }
 ]
}
```

```json
  "id": "Svyatogor adalah bogatyr raksasa dalam bylina Rusia, pahlawan tertua pra-Kristen yang begitu berat sehingga Ibu Bumi tak sanggup menopangnya.",
  "en": "Svyatogor is a giant bogatyr of Russian byliny, the oldest pre-Christian hero, so heavy that Mother Earth cannot bear him.",

 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Svyatogor adalah bogatyr mitis dalam bylina Rusia; namanya berasal dari kata untuk \"gunung suci\". Dalam mitologi Rusia kuno ia bogatyr raksasa, salah satu pahlawan tertua epos bylina yang berada di luar siklus Kiev dan Novgorod. Ia digambarkan \"lebih tinggi dari hutan yang berdiri, lebih rendah dari awan yang berjalan\", tinggal di Pegunungan Suci, dan bila ia berkuda bumi berguncang dan sungai meluap.",
   "en": "Svyatogor is a mythical bogatyr of Russian byliny whose name comes from the words for \"holy mountain\". In old Russian myth he is a giant bogatyr, among the oldest heroes of the epic, standing outside the Kiev and Novgorod cycles. He is \"taller than the standing forest, lower than the drifting cloud\", lives on the Holy Mountains, and when he rides the earth shakes and rivers burst their banks.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "svyatogor-c01",
    "svyatogor-c04",
    "svyatogor-c05"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Menurut epos, Ibu Bumi tak sanggup menahan berat Svyatogor, tetapi ia sendiri tak mampu mengangkat \"tarikan bumi\" yang terkandung dalam sebuah kantong; ketika mencobanya, kakinya terbenam ke tanah. Dalam kisah pertemuannya dengan Ilya Muromets, keduanya menemukan peti mati batu raksasa yang ternyata pas untuk Svyatogor; setelah ia berbaring dan menutupnya, tutup itu tak dapat dibuka lagi, dan sebelum mati ia mewariskan sebagian kekuatannya kepada Ilya lewat napasnya.",
   "en": "The epic says Mother Earth cannot bear Svyatogor's weight, yet he cannot lift the \"pull of the earth\" held in a bag; when he tries, his feet sink into the ground.  "short_description": {
```
```json
  "id": "Babay adalah roh malam (momok) dalam cerita rakyat Slavia yang menurut kepercayaan menculik anak-anak yang tidak tidur atau nakal.",
  "en": "Babay is a night spirit (bogeyman) of Slavic folklore who, by belief, abducts children who do not sleep or misbehave.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "babay-slavic-folklore-c01"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Babay adalah roh malam, semacam momok, dalam cerita rakyat Slavia. Menurut kepercayaan, ia menculik anak-anak yang tidak tidur pada malam hari atau berkelakuan buruk. Ia juga disebut Babayka, Babayko dalam bahasa Ukraina, atau Bobo dalam bahasa Polandia.",
   "en": "Babay is a night spirit, a bogeyman, of Slavic lore. He is believed to carry off children who stay awake at night or misbehave. He is also called Babayka, Babayko in Ukrainian, or Bobo in Polish.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "babay-slavic-folklore-c01"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Babay jarang digambarkan agar anak-anak membayangkannya dalam wujud yang paling menakutkan bagi mereka, tetapi kadang ia lelaki tua hitam bengkok dengan cacat seperti bisu, tak berlengan, atau pincang, membawa karung dan tongkat. Ia dipercaya tinggal di hutan, rawa, atau kebun, berkeliling jalan pada malam hari, dan memasukkan orang yang ditemuinya ke dalam karung; ia mengintip anak-anak dari jendela dan kadang bersembunyi di bawah tempat tidur.",
   "en": "Babay is seldom described, so that each child pictures him in the form they fear most, but at times he is a black, crooked old man with defects such as muteness, armlessness or a limp, carrying a sack and a cane. He is said to live in the forest, a swamp or a garden, to roam the streets by night stuffing anyone he meets into his sack, to peer at children through windows and sometimes to hide under the bed.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "babay-slavic-folklore-c02",
    "babay-slavic-folklore-c03"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Kata babay kemungkinan besar berasal dari bahasa Turkik dan dari bahasa Tatar berarti \"kakek\" atau \"lelaki tua\". Dalam kepercayaan rakyat Polandia, bobo adalah makhluk kecil, buruk rupa, dan jahat yang dipakai untuk menakut-nakuti anak; Peregrynacja dziadowska dari awal abad ke-17 menyebut bobo memukul anak-anak dan merusak rumah.",
   "en": "The word babay is most likely Turkic, meaning \"grandfather\" or \"old man\" in Tatar. In Polish folk belief the bobo was a small, ugly, spiteful being used to frighten children; the early 17th-century Peregrynacja dziadowska says the bobo beat children and caused damage in the house.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "babay-slavic-folklore-c04",
    "babay-slavic-folklore-c05",
    "babay-slavic-folklore-c06"
   ]
  }
 ],

   "source_id": "babay-slavic-folklore-s1",
   "quote": "is a night spirit (Bogeyman) in Slavic folklore. According to beliefs, he abducts children who do not sleep at night or behave badly. He is also called Babayka (Russian: Бабайка), Babayko (Ukrainian: Бабайко) or Bobo (Babok, Bebok) (Polish)",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Babay (Бабай) adalah roh malam (momok) dalam cerita rakyat Slavia yang menculik anak yang tidak tidur atau nakal; juga disebut Babayka, Babayko (Ukraina), atau Bobo (Polandia).",
    "en": "Babay is a night spirit (bogeyman) of Slavic folklore who abducts children who do not sleep or behave badly; also called Babayka, Babayko (Ukrainian) or Bobo (Polish)."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "babay-slavic-folklore-c02",
   "source_id": "babay-slavic-folklore-s1",
   "quote": "Babay is rarely described, so that children can imagine him in the form most terrible for them, but sometimes Babay is described as a pitch-black and crooked old man. He has some physical defects, such as muteness, armlessness, and/or lameness. He has a bag and a cane.",
   "locator": "Role and characteristics",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Babay jarang digambarkan agar anak membayangkannya dalam wujud paling menakutkan, tetapi kadang ia lelaki tua hitam bengkok dengan cacat seperti bisu, tak berlengan, atau pincang, membawa karung dan tongkat; ia tinggal di hutan, rawa, atau kebun.",
    "en": "Babay is rarely described so children imagine their worst form, but sometimes he is a pitch-black crooked old man with defects like muteness, armlessness or lameness, carrying a bag and cane; he lives in the forest, a swamp or a garden."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "babay-slavic-folklore-c03",
   "source_id": "babay-slavic-folklore-s1",
   "quote": "At night, he wanders through the streets and puts into his bag those who meet him on the way. Walking near houses, Babay stands close to the windows and watches the children. ... Also, Babay can sometimes hide under the child's bed, and he may take them if they're awake or get up.",
   "locator": "Role and characteristics",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Pada malam hari ia berkeliling jalan dan memasukkan orang yang ditemuinya ke dalam karung; ia berdiri di dekat jendela mengawasi anak-anak, dan kadang bersembunyi di bawah tempat tidur anak serta mengambilnya bila anak itu bangun.",
    "en": "At night he roams the streets and bags anyone he meets; he stands by windows watching children, and sometimes hides under a child's bed and may take them if they get up."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "babay-slavic-folklore-c04",
   "source_id": "babay-slavic-folklore-s1",
   "quote": "The term babay, alongside its synonym, baba, is most likely of Turkic origin. It is translated as \"grandfather\", \"old man\" from the Tatar language.",
   "locator": "Etymology",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kata babay, bersama sinonimnya baba, kemungkinan besar berasal dari bahasa Turkik dan dari bahasa Tatar diterjemahkan \"kakek\" atau \"lelaki tua\".",
    "en": "The term babay, with its synonym baba, is most likely Turkic, translated from Tatar as \"grandfather\" or \"old man\"."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "babay-slavic-folklore-c05",
   "source_id": "babay-slavic-folklore-s2",
   "quote": "W polskich wierzeniach ludowych bobo był małą, brzydką i złośliwą istotą, którą straszono dzieci w celu ich zdyscyplinowania.",
   "locator": "Historia",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam kepercayaan rakyat Polandia, bobo adalah makhluk kecil, buruk rupa, dan jahat yang dipakai untuk menakut-nakuti anak agar disiplin.",
    "en": "In Polish folk belief the bobo was a small, ugly, spiteful being used to frighten children into discipline."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "babay-slavic-folklore-c06",
   "source_id": "babay-slavic-folklore-s2",
   "quote": "Wzmianka o bobie znajduje się w pochodzącej z początku XVII wieku Peregrynacji dziadowskiej – według niej bobo miał bić dzieci i czynić w domu różne szkody.",
```

```json
   "id": "Bauk adalah makhluk mitis mirip binatang dalam mitologi Serbia dan Slavia Selatan, yang dulu dipakai untuk menakut-nakuti anak kecil. Ia dikatakan bersembunyi di tempat gelap, lubang, atau rumah kosong, menunggu untuk menyergap, membawa pergi, dan melahap korbannya, tetapi dapat diusir dengan cahaya dan bunyi.   "en": "Ileana Cosânzeana is a figure of Romanian mythology, a beautiful, kind princess or a fairy of immense power.",
```

```json
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Ileana Cosânzeana adalah tokoh dalam mitologi Rumania. Ia digambarkan sebagai putri yang cantik dan baik hati, putri seorang kaisar, atau sebagai peri yang sangat sakti. Menurut Nina Cuciuc namanya juga ditulis Cosînzeana, Sânziana, atau Ileana Cosînzeana Sora Soarelui, \"Saudari Matahari\", dan ia juga disebut \"Ratu Bunga\".",
   "en": "Ileana Cosânzeana is a character of Romanian myth, shown as a lovely, kind-hearted princess, an emperor's daughter, or as a fairy of great power. Nina Cuciuc lists spellings such as Cosînzeana, Sânziana and Ileana Cosînzeana Sora Soarelui, \"Sister of the Sun\", and she is also called \"Queen of Flowers\".",
   "claim_ids": [

   "id": "Menurut Adela Ileana Draucean, nama Ileana berasal dari Elena, sedangkan cosânzeana berkaitan dengan sânziană, hari raya Rumania sekaligus sebutan lain bagi peri. Ahli bahasa Sorin Paliga mencatat nama lain seperti Floarea Florilor (\"Bunga segala Bunga\") dan Frumoasa lumii (\"Kecantikan Dunia\"), dan Lazar Saineanu menerjemahkan namanya ke bahasa Prancis sebagai Hélène aux cheveux d'or, \"Helena Berambut Emas\".",
   "en": "Adela Ileana Draucean derives Ileana from Elena and links cosânzeana to sânziană, a Romanian holiday and another word for fairies.  "short_description": {
```
```json
  "id": "Raróg adalah iblis api dalam mitologi Slavia, sering digambarkan sebagai elang api.",
  "en": "The Raróg is a fire demon of Slavic mythology, often depicted as a fiery falcon.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "rarog-c01"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Dalam mitologi Slavia, Raróg atau Raróh adalah iblis api yang sering digambarkan sebagai elang api. Ia roh api yang terkait pemujaan perapian, dibayangkan sebagai burung, biasanya burung pemangsa, atau naga bertubuh berpercikan, juga sebagai pusaran api.",
   "en": "Slavic myth knows the Raróg or Raróh as a fire demon, often shown as a blazing falcon. A spirit of fire tied to the cult of the hearth, it was pictured as a bird, usually a bird of prey, or a dragon with a sparkling body, or a whirl of flame.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "rarog-c01",
    "rarog-c05"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Menurut cerita rakyat Ceko, raroh dapat menetas dari telur yang dierami di atas tungku selama sembilan hari sembilan malam, dan dapat tampil sebagai elang api atau naga. Dalam cerita rakyat Polandia, raróg adalah burung mungil yang muat di saku dan membawa kebahagiaan. Rarášek juga dikatakan mampu berubah menjadi berbagai burung dan binatang; bila memusuhi manusia, ia menghancurkan gandum yang belum dipanen.",
   "en": "Czech lore says a raroh can hatch from an egg kept warm on a stove for nine days and nights, and may appear as a fiery falcon or a dragon. In Polish lore the raróg is a tiny bird that fits in a pocket and brings people happiness. The rarášek is also said to change into various birds and beasts; if hostile to someone, it scatters their unharvested grain to dust.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "rarog-c02",
    "rarog-c06",
    "rarog-c07"
   ]
  }
 ],

   "quote": "According to Czech folklore, a raroh can hatch from an egg that was incubated on a stove for nine days and nights, and can appear either as a fiery falcon or a dragon. In Polish folklore, the rarog is a tiny bird that can be held in a pocket, and can bring people happiness.",

   "id": "rarog-c05",

   "quote": "в славянской мифологии огненный дух, связанный с культом очага. Рарога представляли в образе птицы (обычно хищной) или дракона с искрящимся телом, а также в виде огненного вихря.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam mitologi Slavia Raróg adalah roh api yang terkait pemujaan perapian, dibayangkan sebagai burung (biasanya pemangsa) atau naga bertubuh berpercikan, juga sebagai pusaran api.",
    "en": "In Slavic mythology the Raróg is a fire spirit tied to the hearth cult, pictured as a bird (usually of prey) or a dragon with a sparkling body, or a fiery whirlwind."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "rarog-c06",
   "source_id": "rarog-s2",
   "quote": "Рарашку приписывается способность превращаться в различных птиц и зверей.",
   "locator": "Описание",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Rarášek dikatakan mampu berubah menjadi berbagai burung dan binatang.",
    "en": "The rarášek is credited with turning into various birds and beasts."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "rarog-c07",
   "source_id": "rarog-s2",
   "quote": "Если Рарашек враждебен человеку, он разносит и обращает в прах весь его несжатый хлеб.",
   "locator": "Описание",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Bila Rarášek memusuhi seseorang, ia menghancurkan seluruh gandumnya yang belum dipanen menjadi debu.",
    "en": "If the rarášek is hostile to a person, it scatters all their unharvested grain to dust."
   }
  }
 ],
```

```json
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Samodiva, samovila, atau vila adalah peri, nimfa hutan, atau iblis perempuan berbahaya dalam cerita rakyat Slavia Selatan dan Barat.",
  "en": "The samodiva, samovila or vila is a fairy, wood nymph or harmful female demon of South and West Slavic folklore.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "samodiva-folklore-c01"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Samodiva (jamak samodivi), samovila, atau vila adalah peri, nimfa hutan, atau iblis perempuan yang berbahaya dalam cerita rakyat Slavia Selatan dan Barat. Mereka digambarkan sebagai perempuan cantik berambut panjang pirang atau merah, berbaju putih, dengan kerudung, karangan bunga, atau selempang hijau yang konon memberi mereka kekuatan gaib atau kemampuan terbang; kadang mereka sendiri bersayap.",
   "en": "The samodiva (plural samodivi), samovila or vila is a fairy, wood nymph or dangerous she-demon of South and West Slavic lore. They are pictured as lovely women with long blond or red hair in white dresses, whose veils, wreaths or green sashes are said to give them supernatural power or the power to fly; sometimes they are winged themselves.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "samodiva-folklore-c01",
    "samodiva-folklore-c02"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Kediaman rahasia mereka berada di tempat tinggi, dekat perairan, atau di hutan, misalnya mata air di gunung atau hutan beech di tepi danau tempat mereka mandi. Orang yang melanggar wilayah mereka, terutama menimba air pada malam hari, bisa ditimpa sakit atau maut. Satu tema yang berulang adalah lelaki yang mencuri kerudung atau karangan bunga samodiva untuk menangkapnya dan memaksanya menjadi pengantin.",
   "en": "Their hidden homes lie on heights, by water or in woodland, such as a mountain spring or a beech grove by a lake where they bathe. Anyone trespassing there, especially to draw water at night, may be struck by sickness or death. A recurring theme is a man who steals a samodiva's veil or wreath to capture her and force her to be his bride.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "samodiva-folklore-c02",
    "samodiva-folklore-c03",
    "samodiva-folklore-c04"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Bulgaria mereka jiwa perempuan cantik yang tinggal di gua dekat mata air, padang sunyi, atau kincir angin kosong; malam hari mereka mencuci baju putih yang menyimpan seluruh kekuatan mereka, lalu menari hora di padang terbuka. Kadang mereka membantu panen dan pasangan muda, tetapi lebih sering memusuhi manusia, misalnya menahan mata air.",
   "en": "In Bulgarian lore they are the souls of beautiful women living in caves by springs, wastelands or abandoned windmills; at night they wash the white garments that hold all their power and dance the hora in a glade. Sometimes they help with harvests and young couples, but more often they are hostile, for instance stopping up springs.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "samodiva-folklore-c05",
    "samodiva-folklore-c06",
    "samodiva-folklore-c07",
    "samodiva-folklore-c08"
   ]
  }
 ],
```
