  {
   "id": "samodiva-folklore-c01",
   "source_id": "samodiva-folklore-s1",
   "quote": "are fairies, wood nymphs, or harmful female demons found in South and West Slavic folklore.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Samodiva (самодива, jamak samodivi), samovila, atau vila adalah peri, nimfa hutan, atau iblis perempuan berbahaya dalam cerita rakyat Slavia Selatan dan Barat.",
    "en": "The samodiva (plural samodivi), samovila or vila are fairies, wood nymphs or harmful female demons of South and West Slavic folklore."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "samodiva-folklore-c02",
   "source_id": "samodiva-folklore-s1",
   "quote": "They are described as beautiful women with long flowing tresses of blonde or red hair. They wear white dresses and various other articles of clothing (white veils, wreathes, green sash) said to confer them their supernatural powers (or ability to fly); ... able to rob a samodiva of her veil, wreathe, etc. to capture her, and force her to become his bride",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Mereka perempuan cantik berambut panjang pirang atau merah, berbaju putih dengan kerudung, karangan bunga, atau selempang hijau yang memberi kekuatan gaib atau kemampuan terbang, kadang bersayap; tema berulang: lelaki mencuri kerudung atau karangan bunga samodiva untuk menangkapnya dan memaksanya menjadi pengantin.",
    "en": "They are beautiful women with long blonde or red hair in white dresses, with veils, wreaths or green sashes giving them supernatural power or flight, sometimes winged; a recurring theme is a man robbing a samodiva of her veil or wreath to capture her and force her to be his bride."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "samodiva-folklore-c03",
   "source_id": "samodiva-folklore-s1",
   "quote": "Their secret home occurs at a place associated with high elevation, or bodies of water, or woodland, usually in combination, e.g. a spring on a mountain, or a beechwood grove by a lake where they bathe",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kediaman rahasia mereka berada di tempat tinggi, dekat perairan, atau hutan, biasanya gabungan, misalnya mata air di gunung atau hutan beech di tepi danau tempat mereka mandi.",
    "en": "Their secret home is on heights, by water or in woodland, usually combined, such as a mountain spring or a beech grove by a lake where they bathe."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "samodiva-folklore-c04",
   "source_id": "samodiva-folklore-s1",
   "quote": "Superstition cautions that one should fear trespassing upon their territory, particularly to draw water at night, and the encroacher may be stricken with illness, death, or some other suffering as consequence.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Takhayul memperingatkan agar orang tidak melanggar wilayah mereka, terutama menimba air pada malam hari; pelanggar bisa ditimpa sakit, maut, atau penderitaan lain.",
    "en": "Superstition warns against trespassing on their territory, especially to draw water at night; the trespasser may be struck with illness, death or other suffering."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "samodiva-folklore-c05",
   "source_id": "samodiva-folklore-s2",
   "quote": "Były to dusze pięknych kobiet, zamieszkujące trudno dostępne pieczary przy źródłach wodnych, pustkowia czy opuszczone wiatraki",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Samodiva adalah jiwa perempuan cantik yang tinggal di gua sulit dijangkau dekat mata air, padang sunyi, atau kincir angin kosong.",
    "en": "Samodivas were the souls of beautiful women living in hard-to-reach caves by springs, wastelands or abandoned windmills."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "samodiva-folklore-c06",
   "source_id": "samodiva-folklore-s2",
   "quote": "Samodiwa, samowiła, wiła – demon folkloru bułgarskiego, podobny do wił i jud, pokrewny rusałkom.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Samodiva adalah iblis cerita rakyat Bulgaria, mirip vila dan yuda, berkerabat dengan rusalka.",
    "en": "The samodiva is a demon of Bulgarian folklore, like the vile and yudy, related to rusalki."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "samodiva-folklore-c07",
   "source_id": "samodiva-folklore-s2",
   "quote": "rozbierały się do naga, prały swoje białe szaty, suszyły w blasku księżyca, zazdrośnie ich strzegły, aby nie zostały ukradzione, bowiem w nich tkwiła cała ich moc. Następnie kąpały się, później na polanie obok śpiewały i tańczyły horę.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Malam hari mereka mencuci baju putih, mengeringkannya di bawah cahaya bulan dan menjaganya agar tak dicuri karena di situlah seluruh kekuatan mereka, lalu mandi dan menari hora di padang terbuka.",
    "en": "At night they washed their white garments, dried them in the moonlight and guarded them jealously, for all their power lay in them, then bathed and sang and danced the hora in a nearby glade."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "samodiva-folklore-c08",
   "source_id": "samodiva-folklore-s2",
   "quote": "Samodiwy pojawiały się także jako pracujące kobiety przy zbiorach, wspierały młode małżeństwa z dziećmi. W większości przypadków były jednak nieprzyjazne dla ludzi – wstrzymywały źródła",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Samodiva juga muncul sebagai perempuan yang bekerja saat panen dan membantu pasangan muda beranak, tetapi kebanyakan tidak ramah kepada manusia, misalnya menahan mata air.",
    "en": "Samodivas also appeared as women working at harvest and helped young couples with children, but mostly they were unfriendly to people, stopping springs."
   }
  }
 ],

 "short_description": {
  "id": "Upiór adalah makhluk iblis dari cerita rakyat Slavia dan Turkik, cikal bakal vampir: arwah najis yang merasuki jasad yang membusuk dan membutuhkan darah orang hidup.",
  "en": "The upiór is a demonic being of Slavic and Turkic folklore, the prototype of the vampire: an unclean spirit possessing a decaying body that needs the blood of the living.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "upior-c01",
   "upior-c05"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Upiór adalah makhluk iblis dari cerita rakyat Slavia dan Turkik, cikal bakal vampir. Diduga kepercayaan akan ubır (upiór) menyebar melintasi stepa Eurasia lewat migrasi bangsa Kipchak-Kuman, setelah berasal dari wilayah sekitar Sungai Volga dan stepa Pontus. Kata modern \"vampir\" berasal dari bentuk Slavia Kuno dan Turkik onpyr.",
   "en": "The upiór is a demonic being of Slavic and Turkic lore and the forerunner of the vampire. Belief in the ubır (upiór) is thought to have spread over the Eurasian steppes with the migrations of the Kipchak-Cuman people, having begun around the Volga and the Pontic steppes. The modern word \"vampire\" goes back to the Old Slavic and Turkic form onpyr.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "upior-c01",
    "upior-c02"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Bangsa Slavia percaya bahwa saat seseorang mati, jiwanya keluar dari tubuh dan berkeliaran di sekitar rumah dan tempat kerjanya selama 40 hari, dan selama itu dapat masuk kembali ke jasad. Kematian anak yang belum dibaptis, kematian karena kekerasan atau sebelum waktunya, atau kematian pendosa berat seperti penyihir atau pembunuh dapat membuat jiwa menjadi najis. Vampir adalah perwujudan roh najis yang merasuki jasad yang membusuk; makhluk mayat hidup ini membutuhkan darah orang hidup dan pendendam serta iri kepada mereka.",
   "en": "Slavs believed that at death the soul leaves the body and wanders its home and workplace for 40 days, during which it can re-enter the corpse. The death of an unbaptised child, a violent or untimely death, or the death of a grave sinner such as a sorcerer or murderer could make the soul unclean. A vampire is an unclean spirit possessing a rotting body; this undead creature needs the blood of the living and is vengeful and envious toward them.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "upior-c04",
    "upior-c05",
    "upior-c06"
   ]
  }
 ],

 "places": [],
 "timeline": [],
 "relations": [
  {

 "modern_depictions": [],
 "tradition_vs_modern": null,
 "learning_questions": [
  {
   "id": "Bagaimana kepercayaan tentang jiwa setelah kematian melahirkan kisah vampir?",
   "en": "How did beliefs about the soul after death give rise to vampire stories?"
  }
 ],
 "images": [],
 "sources": [
  {
   "id": "upior-s1",
   "url": "https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upi%C3%B3r",
   "title": "Upiór",

   "published": null,
   "language": "en",
   "type": "wikipedia",
   "accessed": "2026-10-03"
  },
  {
   "id": "upior-s2",
   "url": "https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upi%C3%B3r",
   "title": "Upiór",
   "author": null,
   "publisher": "Wikipedia (es)",
   "published": null,
   "language": "es",
   "type": "wikipedia",
   "accessed": "2026-10-03"
  }
 ],
 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "upior-c01",
   "source_id": "upior-s1",
   "quote": "is a demonic being from Slavic and Turkic folklore, a prototype of the vampire. It is suggested that the ubır (upiór) belief spread across the Eurasian steppes through the migrations of the Kipchak-Cuman people, after having its origins in the regions surrounding the Volga (İtil) River and the Pontic steppes.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Upiór adalah makhluk iblis dari cerita rakyat Slavia dan Turkik, cikal bakal vampir; diduga kepercayaan akan ubır (upiór) menyebar melintasi stepa Eurasia lewat migrasi Kipchak-Kuman, setelah berasal dari sekitar Sungai Volga dan stepa Pontus.",
    "en": "The upiór is a demonic being of Slavic and Turkic folklore, a prototype of the vampire; belief in the ubır (upiór) is suggested to have spread across the Eurasian steppes with the Kipchak-Cuman migrations, originating around the Volga and the Pontic steppes."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "upior-c02",
   "source_id": "upior-s1",
   "quote": "The modern word \"vampire\" derives from the Old Slavic and Turkic form \"онпыр\" (onpyr), with the addition of the sound \"v-\" before a large nasal vowel (on), characteristic of Old Bulgarian",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kata modern \"vampir\" berasal dari bentuk Slavia Kuno dan Turkik onpyr, dengan tambahan bunyi \"v-\" sebelum vokal sengau, ciri bahasa Bulgaria Kuno.",
    "en": "The modern word \"vampire\" derives from the Old Slavic and Turkic form onpyr, with \"v-\" added before a nasal vowel, a feature of Old Bulgarian."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "upior-c03",
   "source_id": "upior-s2",
   "quote": "(lengua tártara: Убыр (Ubır), turco: Ubır, Obur, Obır, (bielorruso moderno: вупыр (vupyr), búlgaro: въпир (văpir), serbio: вампир ( vampir ), checo y eslovaco: upír, Polaco: upiór, wupi, Ruso: упырь (upyr'), ucraniano: упир (upyr)",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Nama upiór dalam berbagai bahasa: Tatar Ubır, Turki Ubır atau Obur, Belarus vupyr, Bulgaria văpir, Serbia vampir, Ceko dan Slovakia upír, Polandia upiór, Rusia upyr', Ukraina upyr.",
    "en": "Names of the upiór across languages: Tatar Ubır, Turkish Ubır or Obur, Belarusian vupyr, Bulgarian văpir, Serbian vampir, Czech and Slovak upír, Polish upiór, Russian upyr', Ukrainian upyr."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "upior-c04",
   "source_id": "upior-s1",
   "quote": "The Slavs believed that upon death the soul would go out of the body and wander about its neighbourhood and workplace for 40 days before moving on to an eternal afterlife.",
   "locator": "In Slavic culture",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Bangsa Slavia percaya saat mati jiwa keluar dari tubuh dan berkeliaran di sekitar rumah dan tempat kerjanya selama 40 hari sebelum ke alam baka; selama itu jiwa dapat masuk kembali ke jasad.",
    "en": "Slavs believed that at death the soul leaves the body and wanders its neighbourhood and workplace for 40 days before going to the afterlife; during this time it could re-enter the corpse."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "upior-c05",
   "source_id": "upior-s1",
   "quote": "A vampire is the manifestation of an unclean spirit possessing a decomposing body. This undead creature needs the blood of the living to sustain its body's existence and is considered to be vengeful and envious towards the living.",
   "locator": "In Slavic culture",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Vampir adalah perwujudan roh najis yang merasuki jasad yang membusuk; makhluk mayat hidup ini membutuhkan darah orang hidup dan dianggap pendendam serta iri kepada mereka.",
    "en": "A vampire is an unclean spirit possessing a decomposing body; this undead creature needs the blood of the living and is vengeful and envious toward them."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "upior-c06",
   "source_id": "upior-s1",
   "quote": "The death of an unbaptized child, a violent or an untimely death, or the death of a grievous sinner (such as a sorcerer or murderer) were all grounds for a soul to become unclean after death.",
   "locator": "In Slavic culture",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kematian anak yang belum dibaptis, kematian karena kekerasan atau sebelum waktunya, atau kematian pendosa berat (penyihir, pembunuh) dapat membuat jiwa menjadi najis.",
    "en": "The death of an unbaptised child, a violent or untimely death, or the death of a grave sinner (sorcerer, murderer) could make a soul unclean."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "upior-c07",
   "source_id": "upior-s1",
   "quote": "In Slavic folk culture, the upiór has features that are strongly present in strzyga",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam budaya rakyat Slavia, upiór punya ciri yang juga kuat pada strzyga.",
    "en": "In Slavic folk culture the upiór has features strongly present in the strzyga."
   }
  }
 ],

  {

   "searched": "Tidak ada gambar Commons yang terbukti menggambarkan upiór tradisional."
  }
 ]

 "traits": [],
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Penyihir Anaga, menurut kepercayaan populer Kepulauan Canaria, adalah perempuan yang mengadakan pertemuan sihir di pegunungan Anaga, Tenerife, dan kemudian dianggap penyihir-vampir pengisap darah bayi.",
  "en": "The Witches of Anaga, by Canarian popular belief, were women holding covens in the Anaga mountains of Tenerife, later held to be witch-vampires who sucked infants' blood.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "witches-of-anaga-c01",
   "witches-of-anaga-c03"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Menurut kepercayaan populer, Penyihir Anaga adalah perempuan yang mengabdi pada perkumpulan sihir di pegunungan Anaga di timur laut Pulau Tenerife, Kepulauan Canaria, Spanyol. Ritual mereka diadakan di punggung pegunungan antara San Andrés dan Taganana, di tempat bernama El Bailadero, yang merujuk pada tarian para penyihir mengelilingi api unggun. Setelah pertemuan itu mereka konon turun ke pantai untuk mandi telanjang.",
   "en": "Popular belief held the Witches of Anaga to be women bound to covens in the Anaga mountains of north-eastern Tenerife, Canary Islands, Spain. Their rites took place on the ridge between San Andrés and Taganana, at a spot called El Bailadero after the witches' dances around a bonfire. After the gatherings they supposedly went down to the coast to bathe naked.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "witches-of-anaga-c01",
    "witches-of-anaga-c02",
    "witches-of-anaga-c06"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Lama-kelamaan, karena pengaruh kisah vampir dari Eropa Timur, mitos itu menambahkan unsur mengisap darah sehingga mereka menjadi penyihir-vampir yang mengisap darah bayi yang tidur di buaian. Ada pula teori bahwa legenda ini berasal dari ritual pagan bangsa Guanche untuk memanggil hujan, yang oleh Gereja Katolik dianggap sihir. Unsur serupa ada di tempat lain di Spanyol, seperti guaxa di Asturias dan guajona di Cantabria.",
   "en": "Over time, under the influence of Eastern European vampire stories, the myth gained a blood-drinking element, turning them into witch-vampires who sucked the blood of babies asleep in their cradles. Another theory traces the legend to Guanche pagan rain rituals that the Catholic Church deemed witchcraft. The same motif appears elsewhere in Spain, as with the guaxa of Asturias and the guajona of Cantabria.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "witches-of-anaga-c03",
    "witches-of-anaga-c04",
    "witches-of-anaga-c05"
   ]
  }
 ],

   "source_id": "witches-of-anaga-s1",
   "quote": "With the passage of time, due to the influence of vampire stories in Eastern Europe, this led to the myth that witches incorporated the aspect of drinking blood, thus making them witch-vampires; typically tales were created that these witches sucked the blood of newborns as they slept in their cribs.",

   "context": "traditional-belief",

   "context": "traditional-belief",

   "source_id": "witches-of-anaga-s1",

  {
   "value": "shapeshifter",

  }
 ],
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Zmeu adalah makhluk fantastis dalam cerita rakyat dan mitologi Rumania, mirip naga tetapi bersifat manusia, yang menculik gadis-gadis dan hampir selalu dikalahkan pangeran pemberani.",
  "en": "The zmeu is a fantastic creature of Romanian folklore and mythology, dragon-like yet human-like, who kidnaps young girls and is almost always defeated by a daring prince.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "zmeu-c01",
   "zmeu-c02",
   "zmeu-c04"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Zmeu (jamak zmei, perempuan zmeoaică) adalah makhluk fantastis dalam cerita rakyat dan mitologi Rumania. Walau sebagian sumber menyebutnya naga, zmeu berbeda karena biasanya jelas bersifat manusia: berkaki dan berlengan, membuat dan memakai senjata, menunggang kuda, dan ingin menikahi gadis muda. Ada pengulas yang menggolongkannya sebagai raksasa (setara ogre), iblis, bahkan vampir.",
   "en": "The zmeu (plural zmei, feminine zmeoaică) is a fantastic being of Romanian folk tales and myth. Some sources call it a dragon, yet it differs in being plainly human-like: it has arms and legs, makes and wields weapons, rides horses and wants to wed young girls. Commentators variously class it as a giant (an ogre), a devil or even a vampire.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "zmeu-c01",
    "zmeu-c02"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Dalam beberapa cerita zmeu muncul di langit dan menyemburkan api atau dapat berubah wujud; dalam cerita lain ia punya batu permata ajaib di kepalanya yang bersinar seperti matahari. Ia menyukai gadis muda yang cantik dan menculiknya, biasanya untuk dinikahi, tetapi hampir selalu dikalahkan oleh pangeran atau kesatria pemberani. Dalam mitologi Rumania ia juga raksasa berkekuatan gaib, perwujudan kejahatan yang selalu kalah oleh kekuatan baik.",
   "en": "In some tales the zmeu appears in the sky spitting fire or can change shape; in others it bears a magic jewel on its head that shines like the sun. It loves beautiful young girls and carries them off, usually to marry them, but is almost always beaten by a bold prince or knight-errant. Romanian myth also makes it a giant with supernatural powers, the embodiment of evil, always overcome by the forces of good.",

   "author": null,

 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "zmeu-c01",
   "source_id": "zmeu-s1",
   "quote": "is a fantastic creature of Romanian folklore and Romanian mythology.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Zmeu (jamak zmei, perempuan zmeoaică) adalah makhluk fantastis dalam cerita rakyat dan mitologi Rumania.",
    "en": "The zmeu (plural zmei, feminine zmeoaică) is a fantastic creature of Romanian folklore and mythology."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "zmeu-c02",
   "source_id": "zmeu-s1",
   "quote": "Though referred by some sources as a dragon, the zmeu is nevertheless distinct, because it usually has clear anthropomorphic traits: it is humanoid and has legs, arms, the ability to create and use artifacts such as weapons, and to ride a horse, and has the desire to marry young girls. There are commentators that class it as a giant (equivalent of an ogre), or a devil, or even a vampire.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Walau sebagian sumber menyebutnya naga, zmeu berbeda karena bersifat manusia: berkaki, berlengan, membuat dan memakai senjata, menunggang kuda, dan ingin menikahi gadis muda; ada pengulas yang menggolongkannya sebagai raksasa (ogre), iblis, atau vampir.",
    "en": "Though some sources call it a dragon, the zmeu is distinct for its human-like traits: legs, arms, making and using weapons, riding a horse and wishing to marry young girls; some commentators class it as a giant (ogre), a devil or a vampire."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "zmeu-c03",
   "source_id": "zmeu-s1",
   "quote": "In some stories, Zmeu appears in the sky and spits fire, or has the ability to change form. In other stories, it has a magical precious stone on its head that shines like the sun.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam beberapa cerita zmeu muncul di langit dan menyemburkan api, atau dapat berubah wujud; dalam cerita lain ia punya batu permata ajaib di kepala yang bersinar seperti matahari.",
    "en": "In some stories the zmeu appears in the sky and spits fire or can change form; in others it has a magic gem on its head shining like the sun."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "zmeu-c04",
   "source_id": "zmeu-s1",
   "quote": "It likes beautiful young girls, whom it kidnaps, usually for the purpose of marrying them. It is almost always defeated by a daring prince or knight-errant.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia menyukai gadis muda yang cantik dan menculiknya, biasanya untuk dinikahi, dan hampir selalu dikalahkan pangeran atau kesatria pemberani.",
    "en": "It likes beautiful young girls and kidnaps them, usually to marry them, and is almost always defeated by a daring prince or knight-errant."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "zmeu-c05",
   "source_id": "zmeu-s1",
   "quote": "Most scholars agree that the Romanian term zmeu derives from Slavic zmey.",
   "locator": "Etymology",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kebanyakan sarjana sepakat istilah Rumania zmeu berasal dari kata Slavia zmey.",
    "en": "Most scholars agree the Romanian term zmeu derives from Slavic zmey."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "zmeu-c06",
   "source_id": "zmeu-s2",
   "quote": "En la mitología rumana, un esmeuro o dracosmeu (en rumano zmeu) es un ser gigante con poderes sobrenaturales, encarnación del mal, pero que siempre es derrotado por las fuerzas del bien",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam mitologi Rumania, zmeu adalah makhluk raksasa berkekuatan gaib, perwujudan kejahatan, yang selalu dikalahkan oleh kekuatan baik.",
    "en": "In Romanian mythology the zmeu is a giant being with supernatural powers, the embodiment of evil, always defeated by the forces of good."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "zmeu-c07",
   "source_id": "zmeu-s2",
   "quote": "La palabra también se refiere a las cometas que vuelan los niños dado que la palabra zmeu también significa cometa en rumano.",
   "locator": "Nombre",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kata zmeu dalam bahasa Rumania juga berarti layang-layang yang diterbangkan anak-anak.",
    "en": "In Romanian the word zmeu also means the kites children fly."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "zmeu-c08",
   "source_id": "zmeu-s1",
   "quote": "The zmeu has also been conflated with or confused with the dracu or with the balaur type dragon. ... like Prâslea cel voinic",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Zmeu juga dikacaukan dengan dracu atau naga jenis balaur; dalam berbagai cerita ia dibunuh pangeran muda seperti Prâslea cel voinic.",
    "en": "The zmeu has also been conflated with the dracu or the balaur dragon; various stories have it slain by a young prince like Prâslea cel voinic."
   }
  }
 ],
 "conflicts": [],
 "gaps": [

   "searched": "Tidak dicari ilustrasi Commons yang terbukti menggambarkan zmeu."
  }
 ]

 "short_description": {
  "id": "Antero Vipunen adalah raksasa dalam mitologi dan lagu rune Finlandia yang terkubur di bawah tanah dan menyimpan mantra serta pengetahuan yang sangat berharga.",
  "en": "Antero Vipunen is a giant of Finnish mythology and runic songs, buried underground and holding very valuable spells and knowledge.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "antero-vipunen-c01"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Antero Vipunen adalah raksasa dalam mitologi dan lagu rune Finlandia. Ia terkubur di bawah tanah dan menyimpan mantra serta pengetahuan yang sangat berharga. Dalam puisi rakyat Balto-Finnik ia orang mati atau raksasa yang berbaring di bawah tanah, seorang tietäjä (dukun bijak) pemilik mantra-mantra purba.",
   "en": "Finnish myth and runic song tell of Antero Vipunen, a giant lying buried beneath the earth who keeps priceless spells and lore. Balto-Finnic folk poetry makes him a dead man or giant asleep underground, a tietäjä (wise sorcerer) holding ancient incantations.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "antero-vipunen-c01",
    "antero-vipunen-c04"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Ketika Väinämöinen membutuhkan tiga kata ajaib (luotteet) untuk menyelesaikan perahunya, ia mendatangi Vipunen dan masuk ke dalam tubuhnya. Di dalam perut Vipunen ia membuat tempat tempa dan memukul-mukul sehingga Vipunen kesakitan, lalu memuntahkannya sambil memberikan kata-kata yang dibutuhkan. Menurut Matti Kuusi, tokoh utama aslinya mungkin Lemminkäinen.",
   "en": "When Väinämöinen needs three magic words (luotteet) to finish his boat, he seeks out Vipunen and ends up inside his body. Inside the giant he builds a forge and hammers away until Vipunen, in pain, spits him out and hands over the words. Matti Kuusi thought the original hero was probably Lemminkäinen.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "antero-vipunen-c02",
    "antero-vipunen-c03"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Dalam bahasa Estonia vibu berarti busur, sehingga di Estonia Vipunen dipahami sebagai pemanah ulung. Dalam banyak versi ia juga disebut pemasang jerat, sesuai namanya: vipu adalah sejenis alat jerat.",
   "en": "In Estonian vibu means a bow, so in Estonia Vipunen was taken to be a skilled archer. Many versions also call him a trapper, as his name suggests: a vipu is a kind of snare.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "antero-vipunen-c05",
    "antero-vipunen-c06"
   ]
  }
 ],

 "short_description": {
  "id": "Boginka adalah roh atau iblis perempuan alam liar dalam mitologi pagan Polandia, sering perwujudan kekuatan alam, yang terkenal menukar bayi manusia.",
  "en": "The boginka is a female spirit or demon of wild nature in Polish pagan mythology, often personifying natural forces, known for swapping human babies.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "boginka-c01",
   "boginka-c03"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Dalam mitologi pagan Polandia, boginki (tunggal boginka) adalah roh atau iblis perempuan alam liar, seperti hutan, ladang, gunung, atau perairan, dan sering merupakan perwujudan kekuatan alam. Kata ini secara harfiah berarti \"dewi kecil\" dan dapat diterjemahkan sebagai peri atau nimfa. Mereka dibayangkan sebagai nenek tua buruk rupa atau gadis muda cantik, biasanya telanjang, dan umumnya jahat.",
   "en": "In Polish pagan myth the boginki (singular boginka) are female spirits or demons of the wild, of forest, field, mountain or water, often embodying natural forces. The name literally means \"little goddess\" and may be rendered as fairy or nymph. They were pictured as hideous crones or lovely young girls, usually naked, and were mostly malevolent.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "boginka-c01"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Istilah boginka kemudian dipakai untuk rusałka, vila, dziwożona, mamuna, dan lainnya. Boginki dapat mencuri bayi manusia dan menggantinya dengan anak pengganti yang disebut odmieniec atau podrzutek. Fungsi utama mereka adalah mengejar perempuan yang baru melahirkan dan perempuan hamil serta menculik dan menukar bayi. Di wilayah Orava dan Spiš mereka digambarkan buruk rupa dengan kepala besar, perut buncit, kaki bengkok, dan payudara besar yang disampirkan ke belakang bahu, dan dapat tampil sebagai kucing, anjing, atau katak.",
   "en": "The word boginka came to cover the rusałka, vila, dziwożona, mamuna and others. Boginki can steal a human baby and leave a substitute called odmieniec or podrzutek. Their chief role is to hound new mothers and pregnant women and to steal and swap babies. In Orava and Spiš they are described as ugly, with big heads, swollen bellies, crooked legs and large breasts thrown back over the shoulder, and they can appear as cats, dogs or frogs.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "boginka-c02",
    "boginka-c03",
    "boginka-c04",
    "boginka-c05"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Menurut kepercayaan rakyat Polandia, perempuan yang mati saat melahirkan, bunuh diri, atau membunuh anaknya menjadi boginka. Mereka tinggal di rawa, danau, sungai, hutan, dan gunung, dan dapat dijumpai di tepi air saat mencuci kain dengan pemukul kayu.",
   "en": "Polish folk belief held that women who died in childbirth, took their own lives or killed their children became boginki. They dwelt in marshes, lakes, rivers, forests and mountains and could be met by the water beating cloth with wooden paddles.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "boginka-c06"
   ]
  }
 ],

 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "boginka-c01",
   "source_id": "boginka-s1",
   "quote": "boginki (singular: boginka) are female spirits or demons of wild nature: forest, field, mountains, or water (both of land and sea) and is often a personification of the forces of nature. The word literally means \"minor goddess\" in Polish and may be translated as \"fairy\" or \"nymph\". They were usually imagined as either ugly old hags or pretty young girls, usually naked. They are usually evil.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam mitologi pagan Polandia, boginki (tunggal boginka) adalah roh atau iblis perempuan alam liar (hutan, ladang, gunung, perairan), sering perwujudan kekuatan alam; kata ini berarti \"dewi kecil\" dan dapat diterjemahkan peri atau nimfa; mereka dibayangkan sebagai nenek buruk rupa atau gadis cantik, biasanya telanjang, dan umumnya jahat.",
    "en": "In Polish pagan mythology boginki (singular boginka) are female spirits or demons of wild nature (forest, field, mountains, water), often personifying natural forces; the word means \"minor goddess\" and may be translated \"fairy\" or \"nymph\"; imagined as ugly hags or pretty girls, usually naked, and usually evil."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "boginka-c02",
   "source_id": "boginka-s1",
   "quote": "Some boginki are rusałka, vila, dziwożona, łaskotałka, mamuna ... or nawka. The term \"boginka\" started to be applied to any of them.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Sebagian boginki adalah rusałka, vila, dziwożona, łaskotałka, mamuna, atau nawka, dan istilah boginka mulai dipakai untuk semuanya.",
    "en": "Some boginki are rusałka, vila, dziwożona, łaskotałka, mamuna or nawka, and the term came to be applied to any of them."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "boginka-c03",
   "source_id": "boginka-s1",
   "quote": "Boginki (mamuny) can steal a human child and substitute them. Such substituted child is called boginiak, płonek , podrzutek,  or odmieniak (\"changeling\").  Often children with mental disabilities were thought to be such.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Boginki (mamuny) dapat mencuri anak manusia dan menggantinya; anak pengganti itu disebut boginiak, płonek, podrzutek, atau odmieniak; anak dengan disabilitas mental sering dianggap anak pengganti.",
    "en": "Boginki (mamuny) can steal a human child and substitute it; the substitute is called boginiak, płonek, podrzutek or odmieniak (\"changeling\"); children with mental disabilities were often thought to be such."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "boginka-c04",
   "source_id": "boginka-s2",
   "quote": "Главные функции богинки — преследование рожениц и беременных, похищение и подмена детей.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Fungsi utama boginka adalah mengejar perempuan yang baru melahirkan dan perempuan hamil, serta menculik dan menukar bayi.",
    "en": "The boginka's main functions are pursuing women in childbirth and pregnant women, and stealing and swapping children."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "boginka-c05",
   "source_id": "boginka-s2",
   "quote": "В поверьях Оравы, Спиша и особенно Замагурья богинка описывается как злое уродливое существо c высокой нескладной фигурой, непропорционально большой головой, вздутым животом, кривыми ногами, чёрными зубами, большими грудями, которые закидывались назад через плечо. Также богинки могут появляться в виде животных — кошек, собак, лягушек и др.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam kepercayaan di Orava dan Spiš, boginka adalah makhluk jahat buruk rupa bertubuh tinggi canggung, berkepala besar, berperut buncit, berkaki bengkok, bergigi hitam, dan berpayudara besar yang disampirkan ke belakang bahu; boginki juga dapat muncul sebagai kucing, anjing, atau katak.",
    "en": "In Orava and Spiš belief the boginka is an evil, ugly being, tall and gawky, with an oversized head, swollen belly, crooked legs, black teeth and large breasts thrown back over the shoulder; boginki can also appear as cats, dogs or frogs."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "boginka-c06",
   "source_id": "boginka-s3",
   "quote": "Boginki zamieszkiwały bagna, jeziora, rzeki, lasy i góry. Można je było spotkać nad wodą podczas prania chust drewnianymi kijankami. ... Boginkami miały stawać się kobiety zmarłe przy porodzie, samobójczynie i morderczynie dzieci.",
   "locator": "Wygląd i działalność",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Perempuan yang mati saat melahirkan, bunuh diri, atau membunuh anaknya dipercaya menjadi boginka; boginki tinggal di rawa, danau, sungai, hutan, dan gunung, dan dijumpai di tepi air saat mencuci kain.",
    "en": "Women who died in childbirth, suicides and child-murderers were said to become boginki; they lived in marshes, lakes, rivers, forests and mountains and were met by the water washing cloths."
   }
  }
 ],

 "short_description": {
  "id": "Ular api (ognennyi zmei) adalah makhluk jahat dalam cerita rakyat Rusia dan Slavia, sering iblis atau roh yang berubah wujud, tampak seperti batang atau bola api yang terbang memercikkan bunga api.",
  "en": "Fiery serpents (ognennyi zmei) are evil beings of Russian and Slavic folklore, often shapeshifted demons or spirits, seen as flying shafts or balls of fire scattering sparks.",

 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Ular api, dalam bahasa Rusia ognennyi zmei dan Ukraina vohnyanyy zmiy, adalah makhluk jahat dalam cerita rakyat Rusia, sering iblis atau roh yang berubah wujud. Dalam tradisi Slavia Timur ia umumnya tampak seperti pikulan menyala, sapu yang terbakar, atau bola api biru yang memercikkan bunga api saat terbang; gambaran ini dikaitkan dengan bola api meteor, bintang jatuh, atau meteorit. Di Rusia ia juga disebut zmey lyubak, nalotnik (\"penyerbu\"), dan letun (\"penerbang\").",
   "en": "The fiery serpent, Russian ognennyi zmei and Ukrainian vohnyanyy zmiy, is a wicked being of Russian folk belief, often a demon or spirit in changed form. In East Slavic tradition it usually looks like a blazing carrying-pole, a burning broom or a glowing ball of blue fire throwing off sparks as it flies; this image has been tied to bolides, shooting stars and meteorites. In Russia it also goes by names such as zmey lyubak, nalotnik (\"raider\") and letun (\"flyer\").",
   "claim_ids": [
    "fiery-serpents-in-slavic-folklore-c01",

    "fiery-serpents-in-slavic-folklore-c03"

  {
   "id": "Ia menjalin hubungan seksual dengan perempuan, yang berakibat sakit dan kematian sang perempuan, dan berwujud ganda: di udara sebagai ular api bersayap, di bumi sebagai manusia. Ular api sering merupakan perwujudan mayat yang datang kepada istri atau perempuan yang dicintainya. Di Ukraina, perelesnyk adalah iblis berwujud ular api (meteor) yang terbang menuju perempuan.",
   "en": "It has sexual relations with women, leading to their illness and death, and has a double form: a fiery winged serpent in the air and a man on the ground.    }
  },
  {
   "id": "fiery-serpents-in-slavic-folklore-c02",
   "source_id": "fiery-serpents-in-slavic-folklore-s1",
   "quote": "In East Slavic tradition, the \"fiery serpent\" is said to generally resemble a fiery shaft (Russian: коромысло, \"fiery carrying pole\"), a flaming broom, or a glowing ball of blue fire, releasing sparks during its flight. This has been connected to the astronomical phenomena of bolides, shooting stars (meteors) or meteorites.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam tradisi Slavia Timur, ular api umumnya mirip batang menyala (\"pikulan api\"), sapu yang terbakar, atau bola api biru yang memercikkan bunga api saat terbang; hal ini dikaitkan dengan bola api meteor, bintang jatuh, atau meteorit.",
    "en": "In East Slavic tradition the fiery serpent generally resembles a fiery shaft (\"fiery carrying pole\"), a flaming broom or a glowing ball of blue fire throwing sparks in flight; this has been linked to bolides, shooting stars or meteorites."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "fiery-serpents-in-slavic-folklore-c03",
   "source_id": "fiery-serpents-in-slavic-folklore-s1",
   "quote": "In Russia, the ognennyi zmei is also known as the zmey lyubak (змей-любак, 'serpent-lyubak'), nalotnik (налётник, 'raider'),  nalot (налёт, 'raid'), letun (летун, 'flyer')",
   "locator": "Nomenclature",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Di Rusia ular api juga disebut zmey lyubak, nalotnik (\"penyerbu\"), nalot, atau letun (\"penerbang\").",
    "en": "In Russia the ognennyi zmei is also called zmey lyubak, nalotnik (\"raider\"), nalot or letun (\"flyer\")."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "fiery-serpents-in-slavic-folklore-c04",
   "source_id": "fiery-serpents-in-slavic-folklore-s1",
   "quote": "In the Ukraine, the perelesnyk (Перелесник, 'tempter, seducer'; var. perevésnyk переве́сник), aka pervonach (Первонач), is a demon in the form of a fiery snake (meteor) that flies towards women.",
   "locator": "Nomenclature",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Di Ukraina, perelesnyk (\"penggoda\") adalah iblis berwujud ular api (meteor) yang terbang menuju perempuan.",
    "en": "In Ukraine the perelesnyk (\"tempter\") is a demon in the form of a fiery snake (meteor) that flies towards women."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "fiery-serpents-in-slavic-folklore-c05",
   "source_id": "fiery-serpents-in-slavic-folklore-s2",
   "quote": "вступает сексуальные отношения с женщинами, результатом чего является болезнь и смерть женщины, также обладает двойственным внешним обликом — в воздухе имеет вид огненного крылатого змея, на земле — человека.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ular api (Огненный змей) menjalin hubungan seksual dengan perempuan, yang berakibat sakit dan kematian sang perempuan, dan berwujud ganda: di udara ular api bersayap, di bumi manusia.",
    "en": "The fiery serpent has sexual relations with women, resulting in their illness and death, and has a double form: a fiery winged serpent in the air, a man on the ground."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "fiery-serpents-in-slavic-folklore-c06",
   "source_id": "fiery-serpents-in-slavic-folklore-s2",
   "quote": "Огненный змей является ипостасью ходячего покойника, после смерти являющегося к своей жене или любимой им женщине.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ular api adalah perwujudan mayat berjalan yang setelah mati datang kepada istrinya atau perempuan yang dicintainya.",
    "en": "The fiery serpent is a guise of a walking dead man who comes after death to his wife or beloved."
   }
  }
 ],

  "id": "Monster Danau Van adalah monster danau dalam cerita rakyat Armenia dan Turki yang konon hidup di Danau Van, Turki timur.",
  "en": "The Lake Van Monster is a lake monster of Armenian and Turkish folklore said to live in Lake Van in eastern Turkey.",
  "claim_ids": [

  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Armenia dan Turki, Monster Danau Van adalah monster danau yang konon tinggal di Danau Van, Turki timur. Penulis kronik Armenia Movses Khorenatsi dan Anania Shirakatsi menulis tentang vishap (naga air dalam mitologi Armenia) yang tinggal di danau itu. Menurut mitos, dewa Vahagn, sang vishapakagh (\"penuai vishap\"), terjun ke Danau Van untuk menyeret keluar vishap yang sudah cukup besar untuk melahap dunia.",
   "en": "Armenian and Turkish folk tradition place the Lake Van Monster in Lake Van, in the east of Turkey. The Armenian chroniclers Movses Khorenatsi and Anania Shirakatsi wrote of vishaps, the water dragons of Armenian myth, living in the lake. In myth the god Vahagn, the vishapakagh (\"reaper of vishaps\"), would dive into Lake Van to haul out any vishap grown big enough to swallow the world.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "lake-van-monster-c01",
    "lake-van-monster-c02",
    "lake-van-monster-c05"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "James Russell mencatat bahwa hingga zaman modern orang Armenia di lembah Van menyebut badai mendadak di danau itu vishap kami, \"angin naga\". Surat kabar Utsmaniyah Saadet pada 29 April 1889 memberitakan makhluk yang konon menyeret seorang lelaki ke danau; pemerintah Utsmaniyah lalu mengirim tim survei ilmiah, yang tidak berhasil melihat makhluk itu. Russell menganggap kisah Vahagn sebagai adopsi Armenia atas mitos Urartu tentang dewa Teisheba yang melawan monster air.",
   "en": "James Russell notes that into modern times the Armenians of the Van basin called the sudden storms on the lake vishap kami, \"dragon wind\". The Ottoman newspaper Saadet reported on 29 April 1889 that a creature had dragged a man into the lake; the Ottoman government sent a scientific survey, which failed to spot it. Russell sees the Vahagn story as an Armenian borrowing of Urartian myths of the god Teisheba fighting a water monster.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "lake-van-monster-c03",
    "lake-van-monster-c04",
    "lake-van-monster-c06"
   ]

  "id": "Lempo adalah iblis dalam cerita rakyat dan mitologi Finlandia yang dikaitkan dengan cinta dan api, dan kemudian disamakan dengan Iblis oleh Kekristenan.",
  "en": "Lempo is a demon of Finnish folklore and mythology linked with love and fire, later identified with the Devil by Christianity.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "lempo-c01",

 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Lempo adalah iblis dalam cerita rakyat dan mitologi Finlandia. Namanya dikaitkan dengan Lemmes, Lemmas, Lemmätär, dan Lemmetär (\"Nyonya Cinta\"), serta dengan tema cinta dan api. Nama berakhiran -tär bersifat perempuan, tetapi Lempo kadang juga dipahami sebagai iblis laki-laki. Dalam kepercayaan kuno Finlandia ia makhluk kepercayaan atau dewa yang oleh Kekristenan disamakan dengan Iblis.",
   "en": "Finnish folklore and myth know Lempo as a demon. The name is linked to Lemmes, Lemmas, Lemmätär and Lemmetär (\"Lady Love\") and to the themes of love and fire. The -tär names are feminine, yet Lempo has at times been seen as a male demon. In old Finnish belief Lempo was a being or god whom Christianity equated with the Devil.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "lempo-c01",
    "lempo-c04"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Christfried Ganander menyebut Lempo roh jahat yang terbang dan menempa panah-panah jahat yang terbang. Matthias Castrén mengaitkan nama itu dengan kata lempi, \"cinta erotis\"; dalam mantra cinta, Lempi bisa menjadi perasaan liar yang mengamuk, sehingga Castrén menduga dewa cinta dapat berubah menjadi dewa jahat. Elias Lönnrot mengaitkannya dengan kata Swedia slem, \"buruk\". Umpatan ringan \"Lempo soikoon!\" semula adalah seruan kepada Lempo.",
   "en": "In Christfried Ganander's account Lempo was an airborne spirit of evil who made wicked arrows that flew. Matthias Castrén linked the name to lempi, \"erotic love\"; in love spells Lempi could be a wild, raging feeling, so Castrén thought a love god could have turned into an evil one. Elias Lönnrot connected it with the Swedish slem, \"lousy\". The mild curse \"Lempo soikoon!\" was originally an appeal to Lempo.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "lempo-c02",
    "lempo-c03",
    "lempo-c05",
    "lempo-c06"
   ]
  }
 ],

 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "lempo-c01",
   "source_id": "lempo-s1",
   "quote": "is a demon from Finnish folklore and mythology. Lempo has been connected to the names Lemmes, Lemmas, Lemmätär and Lemmetär ... as well as themes of love and fire. The -tär ending names are feminine, but Lempo has also been understood as a male demon in some instances.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Lempo adalah iblis dalam cerita rakyat dan mitologi Finlandia, dikaitkan dengan nama Lemmes, Lemmas, Lemmätär, dan Lemmetär (\"Nyonya Cinta\") serta tema cinta dan api; nama berakhiran -tär bersifat perempuan, tetapi Lempo kadang dipahami sebagai iblis laki-laki.",
    "en": "Lempo is a demon of Finnish folklore and mythology linked to the names Lemmes, Lemmas, Lemmätär and Lemmetär (\"Lady Love\") and to love and fire; the -tär names are feminine, but Lempo has also been seen as a male demon."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "lempo-c02",
   "source_id": "lempo-s1",
   "quote": "Christfried Ganander called Lempo a flying evil spirit who forged evil flying arrows",
   "locator": "Descriptions",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Christfried Ganander menyebut Lempo roh jahat yang terbang dan menempa panah-panah jahat yang terbang.",
    "en": "Christfried Ganander called Lempo a flying evil spirit who forged evil flying arrows."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "lempo-c03",
   "source_id": "lempo-s1",
   "quote": "Matthias Castrén connected the name Lempo to the word lempi 'erotic love'. ... so Castrén thought a love god could have thus developed into an evil god. Elias Lönnrot connected the name to the Swedish word slem 'lousy'.",
   "locator": "Descriptions",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Matthias Castrén mengaitkan nama Lempo dengan lempi, \"cinta erotis\", dan menduga dewa cinta dapat berkembang menjadi dewa jahat; Elias Lönnrot mengaitkannya dengan kata Swedia slem, \"buruk\".",
    "en": "Matthias Castrén linked Lempo to lempi, \"erotic love\", and thought a love god could have become an evil god; Elias Lönnrot linked it to the Swedish slem, \"lousy\"."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "lempo-c04",
   "source_id": "lempo-s2",
   "quote": "Lempo on suomalaisessa muinaisuskossa uskomusolento tai jumala, jonka kristinusko samaisti paholaiseen.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam kepercayaan kuno Finlandia, Lempo adalah makhluk kepercayaan atau dewa yang oleh Kekristenan disamakan dengan Iblis.",
    "en": "In old Finnish belief Lempo is a mythical being or god whom Christianity identified with the Devil."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "lempo-c05",
   "source_id": "lempo-s1",
   "quote": "This lempi in spells could be a wild and raging feeling, like lust awoken by an evil witch",
   "locator": "Descriptions",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam mantra, lempi bisa menjadi perasaan liar yang mengamuk, seperti nafsu yang dibangkitkan penyihir jahat.",
    "en": "In spells this lempi could be a wild, raging feeling, like lust roused by an evil witch."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "lempo-c06",
   "source_id": "lempo-s2",
   "quote": "Lievä kirous ”Lempo soikoon!” on ollut alun perin vetoamista Lempoon",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Umpatan ringan \"Lempo soikoon!\" semula adalah seruan kepada Lempo.",
    "en": "The mild curse \"Lempo soikoon!\" was originally an appeal to Lempo."
   }
  }
 ],

   "source_id": "moroi-s1",
   "quote": "Moroi are often associated with other figures in Romanian folklore, such as strigoi (another type of vampire), vârcolac (werewolf), or pricolici (werewolf). As with most concepts in folklore, the exact characteristics ascribed to moroi are variable from source to source.",

   "source_id": "moroi-s2",
   "quote": "Литература, основанная на фольклоре, объясняет различие между мороем и другой разновидностью вампира — стригоем — так: морои являются живыми вампирами, а стригои — вампирами, восставшими из мёртвых. Хотя в румынском фольклоре это различие не всегда соблюдено.",

 "short_description": {
  "id": "Žaltys (\"ular rumput\") adalah roh rumah dalam mitologi Lituania, binatang suci dewi matahari Saulė, penjaga rumah dan lambang kesuburan.",
  "en": "The žaltys (\"grass snake\") is a household spirit of Lithuanian mythology, sacred animal of the sun goddess Saulė, guardian of the home and symbol of fertility.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "zaltys-c01"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Žaltys, harfiah \"ular rumput\", adalah roh rumah dalam mitologi Lituania. Ia binatang suci dewi matahari Saulė dan dianggap penjaga rumah serta lambang kesuburan. Orang memeliharanya di dekat tungku atau tempat khusus di rumah, percaya ia membawa panen baik dan kekayaan; membunuhnya dipercaya mendatangkan malapetaka besar. Bila ditemukan di ladang, ia diberi susu agar mau menjadi hewan rumah yang suci.",
   "en": "The žaltys, literally \"grass snake\", is a house spirit of Lithuanian myth. It is the sacred animal of the sun goddess Saulė and was seen as guardian of the home and a sign of fertility. People kept it by the stove or in a special corner, trusting it to bring good harvests and wealth; killing one was thought to bring great disaster. One found in a field was offered milk to win it over as a sacred house pet.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "zaltys-c01"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Dalam satu kisah, gadis bernama Eglė menemukan žaltys di pakaiannya setelah mandi di danau; ular itu berjanji mengembalikan pakaiannya bila Eglė mau menikahinya. Ia ternyata Žilvinas, Raja Ular Rumput, yang menjelma pemuda tampan dan membawa Eglė ke rumahnya di bawah laut. Dalam mitologi Latvia dan Lituania, žaltys juga pelindung ternak dan utusan para dewa; ada pepatah Lituania, \"Melihat ular rumput mati, Matahari pun menangis\".",
   "en": "In one story a girl named Eglė finds a žaltys in her clothes after bathing in a lake; it promises to return them if she marries it. It turns out to be Žilvinas, King of the Grass Snakes, who becomes a handsome young man and takes her to his home under the sea. In Latvian and Lithuanian myth the žaltys also protects cattle and is a messenger of the gods; a Lithuanian proverb says that even the Sun weeps at the sight of a dead grass snake.",

 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "zaltys-c01",
   "source_id": "zaltys-s1",
   "quote": "is a household spirit in Lithuanian mythology. It is a sacred animal of the sun goddess Saulė, and was considered a guardian of the home and a symbol of fertility. People used to keep it as a pet by the stove or other special area of the house, believing that it would bring good harvest and wealth. Killing the žaltys was said to bring great misfortunes upon the household.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Žaltys (ular rumput) adalah roh rumah dalam mitologi Lituania, binatang suci dewi matahari Saulė, penjaga rumah dan lambang kesuburan; orang memeliharanya di dekat tungku dan percaya ia membawa panen baik dan kekayaan; membunuhnya mendatangkan malapetaka; bila ditemukan di ladang ia diberi susu.",
    "en": "The žaltys (grass snake) is a household spirit of Lithuanian mythology, sacred animal of the sun goddess Saulė, guardian of the home and symbol of fertility; people kept it by the stove believing it brought good harvests and wealth; killing it brought misfortune; one found in a field was given milk."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "zaltys-c02",
   "source_id": "zaltys-s1",
   "quote": "There is a story of a young women named Eglė who found a žaltys in her clothes after she bathed in a lake. He spoke to her and said he would give her back her clothes if she married him. She eventually agreed, and later the žaltys, who was King of the Grass Snakes Žilvinas, would take the form of a handsome young man to become her husband.",
   "locator": "Legends",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ada kisah gadis bernama Eglė yang menemukan žaltys di pakaiannya setelah mandi di danau; ular itu berjanji mengembalikan pakaiannya bila ia mau menikahinya; Eglė setuju, dan žaltys itu, Žilvinas, Raja Ular Rumput, menjelma pemuda tampan menjadi suaminya.",
    "en": "There is a story of a young woman named Eglė who found a žaltys in her clothes after bathing in a lake; it offered to return them if she married it; she agreed, and the žaltys, Žilvinas, King of the Grass Snakes, became a handsome young man and her husband."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "zaltys-c03",
   "source_id": "zaltys-s1",
   "quote": "He took her to his undersea home where she became immortal as Queen of the Grass Snakes.",
   "locator": "Legends",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia membawa Eglė ke rumahnya di bawah laut, tempat Eglė menjadi abadi sebagai Ratu Ular Rumput.",
    "en": "He took her to his undersea home, where she became immortal as Queen of the Grass Snakes."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "zaltys-c04",
   "source_id": "zaltys-s2",
   "quote": "Жалтис считался посланцем богов, любимцем Солнца, потому убийство Жалтиса приравнивалось к преступлению. В литовском языке существует пословица: «При виде мёртвого ужа и Солнце плачет».",
   "locator": "Змеиный культ",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Žaltys dianggap utusan para dewa dan kesayangan Matahari, sehingga membunuhnya disamakan dengan kejahatan; pepatah Lituania berkata \"Melihat ular rumput mati, Matahari pun menangis\".",
    "en": "The žaltys was considered a messenger of the gods and the Sun's favourite, so killing it was a crime; a Lithuanian proverb says \"At the sight of a dead grass snake even the Sun weeps\"."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "zaltys-c05",
   "source_id": "zaltys-s2",
   "quote": "божество в образе ужа в латышской и литовской мифологии.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Žaltys (Latvia: Zalktis) adalah dewa berwujud ular rumput dalam mitologi Latvia dan Lituania.",
    "en": "The žaltys (Latvian Zalktis) is a deity in the form of a grass snake in Latvian and Lithuanian mythology."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "zaltys-c06",
   "source_id": "zaltys-s2",
   "quote": "Жальтис считался покровителем скота и сравнивается с Piena Māte (Молочная мать).",
   "locator": "Змеиный культ",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Žaltys dianggap pelindung ternak dan dibandingkan dengan Piena Māte (Ibu Susu).",
    "en": "The žaltys was considered a protector of livestock and compared with Piena Māte (the Milk Mother)."
   }
  }
 ],

 "short_description": {
  "id": "Blud adalah roh jahat dalam mitologi Slavia yang menyesatkan orang sehingga mereka berputar-putar tanpa arah.",
  "en": "Blud is an evil spirit of Slavic mythology that confuses people and leads them aimlessly round and round.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "blud-c01",
   "blud-c02"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Blud adalah salah satu makhluk dalam mitologi Slavia, termasuk Ukraina: roh jahat yang membuat orang kehilangan arah dan menuntun mereka berputar-putar tanpa tujuan. Ia termasuk golongan roh najis yang mengelabui para pelancong, menyesatkan mereka, dan membawa mereka ke dalam bahaya. Kata blud juga merujuk pada perzinaan, yang menurut para rohaniwan Slavia hasratnya berasal dari Iblis.",
   "en": "Slavic myth, Ukrainian included, knows the blud as a malign spirit that throws people off course and leads them round in circles. It is one of the unclean powers that deceives travellers, turns them from their road and draws them into danger. The word also denotes illicit fornication, a desire Slavic clerics said came from the Devil.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "blud-c01",
    "blud-c02"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Menurut cerita, blud adalah malaikat yang dibuang Tuhan dari surga. Malaikat lain yang jatuh ke bumi menjadi setan, roh hutan, roh air, dan roh najis lainnya, tetapi blud belum sempat mendarat dan tergantung di udara ketika Tuhan berkata \"Amin\". Karena itu ia menempel pada setiap orang yang tak sengaja menyentuhnya, menggelapkan pikirannya, mengirim penglihatan palsu, atau memanggil dengan suara.",
   "en": "Legend says the blud is an angel God cast out of heaven. The other angels that fell to earth became devils, forest spirits, water spirits and other unclean beings, but the blud had not yet landed and was left hanging in the air when God said \"Amen\". So it clings to anyone who brushes against it, clouding their mind, sending false visions or calling out.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "blud-c03",
    "blud-c04"
   ]
  }
 ],

  {
   "id": "blud-c01",
   "source_id": "blud-s1",
   "quote": "one of the Slavic fairies in Slavic mythology, is an evil-deity that causes disorientation and leads a person aimlessly around and round. The term also refers to illicit fornication, the desire for which Slavic clerics claimed to come from the Devil.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Blud (Блуд) dalam mitologi Slavia adalah roh jahat yang membuat orang kehilangan arah dan menuntunnya berputar-putar tanpa tujuan; istilah ini juga berarti perzinaan, yang hasratnya menurut rohaniwan Slavia berasal dari Iblis.",
    "en": "Blud in Slavic mythology is an evil being that causes disorientation and leads a person aimlessly round and round; the term also means illicit fornication, whose desire Slavic clerics said came from the Devil."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "blud-c02",
   "source_id": "blud-s2",
   "quote": "Блуд — персонаж слов'янської міфології, зокрема й української, різновид нечистої сили, що вводить в оману подорожніх, змушує їх блукати, збиває зі шляху, заводить у небезпеку.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Blud adalah tokoh mitologi Slavia, termasuk Ukraina, sejenis roh najis yang mengelabui pelancong, membuat mereka tersesat, menyimpang dari jalan, dan masuk ke bahaya.",
    "en": "Blud is a figure of Slavic, including Ukrainian, mythology, a kind of unclean force that deceives travellers, makes them wander, leads them off the road and into danger."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "blud-c03",
   "source_id": "blud-s2",
   "quote": "За переказами, блуд — це скинутий Богом з неба янгол (слуга Сатани). Інші янголи стали чортами, лісовиками, водяниками та іншою нечистою силою, коли впали на землю. Блуд не встиг приземлитися та завис у повітрі після того, як Бог промовив «Амінь».",
   "locator": "Образ блуду",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut cerita, blud adalah malaikat (pelayan Setan) yang dibuang Tuhan dari surga; malaikat lain yang jatuh ke bumi menjadi setan, roh hutan, roh air, dan roh najis lain, tetapi blud belum sempat mendarat dan tergantung di udara saat Tuhan berkata \"Amin\".",
    "en": "By legend the blud is an angel (servant of Satan) cast from heaven by God; the other angels became devils, forest and water spirits and other unclean beings on falling to earth, but the blud had not landed and hung in the air when God said \"Amen\"."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "blud-c04",
   "source_id": "blud-s2",
   "quote": "Відтак блуд чіпляється до кожного перехожого, який випадково доторкнеться до нього. Він змушує людину блукати, затьмарюючи розум, насилаючи оманливі видіння чи кличучи голосом.",
   "locator": "Образ блуду",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Karena itu blud menempel pada setiap orang yang tak sengaja menyentuhnya, membuatnya tersesat dengan menggelapkan pikiran, mengirim penglihatan palsu, atau memanggil dengan suara.",
    "en": "So the blud clings to any passer-by who touches it by chance, making them wander by clouding the mind, sending false visions or calling with a voice."
   }
  }
 ],

 "short_description": {
  "id": "Kutkh adalah roh Gagak yang dihormati berbagai masyarakat adat Timur Jauh Rusia, tokoh penciptaan, leluhur manusia, dukun sakti, sekaligus penipu.",
  "en": "Kutkh is a Raven spirit revered by various indigenous peoples of the Russian Far East, a creation figure, ancestor of mankind, mighty shaman and trickster.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "kutkh-c01"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Kutkh, juga Kutkha atau Kutq, adalah roh Gagak yang secara tradisional dihormati dalam berbagai bentuk oleh masyarakat adat Timur Jauh Rusia. Ia muncul dalam banyak legenda sebagai tokoh kunci penciptaan, leluhur manusia yang subur, dukun sakti, dan penipu. Ia tokoh populer dalam cerita orang Chukchi dan berperan sentral dalam mitologi orang Koryak dan Itelmen di Kamchatka.",
   "en": "Kutkh, also Kutkha or Kutq, is a Raven spirit honoured in various guises by the native peoples of the Russian Far East. Legends cast him as a key creator figure, a fertile forefather of humankind, a powerful shaman and a trickster. He is a favourite of Chukchi tales and central to the myths of the Koryaks and Itelmens of Kamchatka.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "kutkh-c01"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Di kalangan Chukchi ia disebut Kúrkil, di kalangan Itelmen Kutq, dan dalam bahasa Koryak namanya sering dipakai dalam bentuk pembesar seperti KutqÍnnaku, \"Kutkh Besar\", yang sering diterjemahkan \"Tuhan\". Kisah-kisahnya beragam dan sering bertentangan: dalam sebagian ia diciptakan oleh Sang Pencipta dan membawa fajar ke bumi, dalam kisah lain ia menciptakan dirinya sendiri. Banyak kisahnya mirip cerita Gagak masyarakat adat Pantai Barat Laut Pasifik, yang menunjukkan kontak budaya tak langsung yang panjang antara Asia dan Amerika Utara.",
   "en": "The Chukchi call him Kúrkil, the Itelmens Kutq, and in Koryak the name is often used in an augmentative form such as KutqÍnnaku, \"Big Kutkh\", often translated \"God\". His tales come in many, often contradictory versions: in some a Creator makes him and he lets dawn onto the earth, in others he creates himself. Many resemble Raven stories of the Pacific Northwest Coast peoples, suggesting long indirect cultural contact between Asia and North America.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "kutkh-c02",
    "kutkh-c03",
    "kutkh-c04",
    "kutkh-c05"
   ]
  }
 ],

   "id": "kutkh-c01",

   "quote": "is a Raven spirit traditionally revered in various forms by various indigenous peoples of the Russian Far East. Kutkh appears in many legends: as a key figure in creation, as a fertile ancestor of mankind, as a mighty shaman and as a trickster. He is a popular subject of the animist stories of the Chukchi people and plays a central role in the mythology of the Koryaks and Itelmens of Kamchatka.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kutkh (juga Kutkha, Кутх) adalah roh Gagak yang dihormati berbagai masyarakat adat Timur Jauh Rusia; dalam banyak legenda ia tokoh kunci penciptaan, leluhur manusia, dukun sakti, dan penipu; populer dalam cerita orang Chukchi dan sentral dalam mitologi Koryak dan Itelmen di Kamchatka.",
    "en": "Kutkh (also Kutkha) is a Raven spirit revered by various indigenous peoples of the Russian Far East; in many legends a key creation figure, ancestor of mankind, mighty shaman and trickster; popular in Chukchi stories and central to Koryak and Itelmen mythology in Kamchatka."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "kutkh-c02",
   "source_id": "kutkh-s1",
   "quote": "Regionally, he is known as Kúrkil among the Chukchi; as Kutq among the Itelmens",
   "locator": "Names",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Di kalangan Chukchi ia disebut Kúrkil, di kalangan Itelmen Kutq.",
    "en": "Regionally he is Kúrkil among the Chukchi and Kutq among the Itelmens."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "kutkh-c03",
   "source_id": "kutkh-s1",
   "quote": "In Koryak, the name is employed commonly in its augmentative form, (KutqÍnnaku, KusqÍnnaku, KuyÍnnaku) all meaning \"Big Kutkh\" and often translated simply as \"God\".",
   "locator": "Names",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam bahasa Koryak namanya sering dipakai dalam bentuk pembesar (KutqÍnnaku dan lain-lain) yang berarti \"Kutkh Besar\" dan sering diterjemahkan \"Tuhan\".",
    "en": "In Koryak the name is often used in augmentative form (KutqÍnnaku and others) meaning \"Big Kutkh\", often translated \"God\"."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "kutkh-c04",
   "source_id": "kutkh-s1",
   "quote": "The tales of Kutkh come in many, often contradictory versions. In some tales he is explicitly created by a Creator and lets the dawn onto the earth by chipping away at the stones surrounding her. In others he creates himself",
   "locator": "Myths",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kisah Kutkh punya banyak versi yang sering bertentangan: dalam sebagian ia diciptakan Sang Pencipta dan membawa fajar ke bumi dengan memecahkan batu yang mengurungnya; dalam versi lain ia menciptakan dirinya sendiri.",
    "en": "Kutkh's tales come in many contradictory versions: in some a Creator makes him and he lets the dawn onto the earth by chipping away the stones around her; in others he creates himself."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "kutkh-c05",
   "source_id": "kutkh-s1",
   "quote": "Many of the stories regarding Kutkh are similar to those of the Raven among the indigenous peoples of the Pacific Northwest Coast, suggesting a long history of indirect cultural contact between Asian and North American peoples.",
   "locator": "lead",

    "en": "Many Kutkh stories resemble Raven tales of the Pacific Northwest Coast peoples, suggesting long indirect cultural contact between Asia and North America."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "kutkh-c06",
   "source_id": "kutkh-s2",
   "quote": "Кутх или Кутха — воплощение духа Ворона, традиционно почитающееся различными коренными народностями на Дальнем Востоке России.",

 "short_description": {
  "id": "Nocnitsa adalah roh atau iblis mimpi buruk dalam mitologi Slavia yang menyiksa orang, terutama anak-anak, pada malam hari.",
  "en": "The nocnitsa is a nightmare spirit or demon of Slavic mythology that torments people, especially children, at night.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "nocnitsa-c01"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Dalam mitologi Slavia, nocnitsa, yang sering disebut dalam bentuk jamak, adalah roh atau iblis mimpi buruk yang menyiksa orang, terutama anak-anak, pada malam hari. Ia juga disebut kriksy, plaksy, atau plachky dalam bahasa Slavia Timur. Nochnitsy adalah roh malam perempuan yang memusuhi manusia; mereka menyerang anak-anak yang hendak tidur sehingga gelisah dan tak bisa tidur, dan bangsa Slavia percaya tangis malam dan tidur yang buruk pada anak adalah ulah mereka.",
   "en": "In Slavic myth the nocnitsa, usually spoken of in the plural, is a nightmare spirit or demon that plagues people, children above all, at night. East Slavic names include kriksy, plaksy and plachky. The nochnitsy are female night spirits hostile to people; they set on children going to sleep, leaving them restless and sleepless, and Slavs blamed children's night crying and poor sleep on them.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "nocnitsa-c01",
    "nocnitsa-c02"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Wujudnya beragam menurut daerah: di Serbia timur nenek buruk rupa berbaju hitam, menurut kepercayaan Kroasia perempuan berambut panjang dengan cakar di jari, dan menurut sedikit data Polandia mirip burung atau kelelawar. Di Silesia, nocnica adalah iblis perempuan yang mencekik orang yang tidur, terutama bayi, dan membawa penyakit; ia disebut dalam Katalog Sihir Bruder Rudolf dari abad ke-13.",
   "en": "Its look varies by region: in eastern Serbia an ugly old woman in black, in Croatian belief a long-haired woman with clawed fingers, and in scant Polish data something like a bird or bat. In Silesia the nocnica was a female demon who choked sleepers, especially infants, and brought disease; it is mentioned in Brother Rudolf's 13th-century Catalogue of Magic.",

    "nocnitsa-c03",

   ]
  }

  "en": "The psoglav (\"dog-head\") is a demonic creature of Balkan mythology with a human body, horse legs, a dog's head with iron teeth and one eye on its forehead.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "psoglav-c01"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Psoglav, harfiah \"kepala anjing\", adalah makhluk mitis bersifat iblis dalam mitologi Balkan; kepercayaan kepadanya ada di sebagian Bosnia dan Montenegro. Ia digambarkan bertubuh manusia dengan kaki kuda, berkepala anjing bergigi besi, dan bermata satu di dahi, seperti kiklop Yunani.",
   "en": "The psoglav, literally \"dog-head\", is a demonic mythical being of Balkan myth; people in parts of Bosnia and Montenegro believed in it. Its body is human but its legs are a horse's; its head is a dog's, armed with iron teeth, and like the Greek cyclops it has one eye set in its brow.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "psoglav-c01",
    "psoglav-c03",
    "psoglav-c05"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Psoglav dikatakan tinggal di gua atau di negeri gelap yang kaya batu permata tetapi tanpa matahari. Mereka memakan manusia, bahkan menggali kuburan untuk memakan mayat. Dalam bahasa Kroasia disebut psoglavac dan Slovenia psoglavec, dan banyak legenda tentangnya terutama di Semenanjung Istria. Vuk Karadžić dalam kamus Serbia tahun 1818 mencatat kata psoglav dengan arti \"manusia berkepala anjing\".",
   "en": "Psoglavs are said to live in caves or a dark land rich in gemstones but without sun. They eat people and even dig up graves to devour corpses. Croatian calls them psoglavac and Slovene psoglavec, and many legends about them come from the Istrian peninsula. Vuk Karadžić's 1818 Serbian dictionary records psoglav as \"a man with a dog's head\".",
   "claim_ids": [
    "psoglav-c02",
    "psoglav-c04",
    "psoglav-c06"
   ]
  }
 ],

 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "psoglav-c01",
   "source_id": "psoglav-s1",
   "quote": "(Serbian Cyrillic: Псоглав , literally \"doghead\") is a demonic mythical creature in Balkan mythology; belief about it existed in parts of Bosnia and Montenegro. Psoglav was described as having a human body with horse legs, a dog's head with iron teeth, and a single eye on the forehead.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Psoglav (Псоглав, harfiah \"kepala anjing\") adalah makhluk mitis bersifat iblis dalam mitologi Balkan yang dipercaya di sebagian Bosnia dan Montenegro, bertubuh manusia berkaki kuda, berkepala anjing bergigi besi, dan bermata satu di dahi.",
    "en": "The psoglav (\"doghead\") is a demonic creature of Balkan mythology believed in parts of Bosnia and Montenegro, with a human body and horse legs, a dog's head with iron teeth and one eye on the forehead."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "psoglav-c02",
   "source_id": "psoglav-s1",
   "quote": "Psoglavs were described as living in caves or in a dark land which has plenty of gemstones, but no sun. They practice anthropophagy, by eating people, or even digging out corpses from graves to eat them. In Croatian the term is psoglavac, and in Slovene it is psoglavec. There are numerous legends about them, particularly on the Istrian peninsula in Croatia.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Psoglav tinggal di gua atau negeri gelap yang kaya permata tanpa matahari, memakan manusia, bahkan menggali kuburan untuk memakan mayat; dalam bahasa Kroasia disebut psoglavac dan Slovenia psoglavec; banyak legenda tentangnya terutama di Semenanjung Istria, Kroasia.",
    "en": "Psoglavs live in caves or a dark, gem-rich, sunless land, eat people and even dig up corpses; Croatian psoglavac, Slovene psoglavec; many legends exist, especially on the Istrian peninsula in Croatia."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "psoglav-c03",
   "source_id": "psoglav-s2",
   "quote": "Ово биће је описивано као химера са људским телом, коњским ногама и псећом главом. Имало је гвоздене зубе и једно око, попут грчких киклопа.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Makhluk ini digambarkan sebagai kimera bertubuh manusia, berkaki kuda, dan berkepala anjing, bergigi besi dan bermata satu seperti kiklop Yunani.",
    "en": "This being was described as a chimera with a human body, horse legs and a dog's head, with iron teeth and one eye like the Greek cyclopes."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "psoglav-c04",
   "source_id": "psoglav-s2",
   "quote": "Вук Караџић је у Српском рјечнику из 1818. записао реч псоглав у значењу: „човек са главом пса”.",
   "locator": "Име",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Vuk Karadžić dalam Kamus Serbia tahun 1818 mencatat kata psoglav dengan arti \"manusia berkepala anjing\".",
    "en": "Vuk Karadžić recorded psoglav in his 1818 Serbian Dictionary as \"a man with a dog's head\"."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "psoglav-c05",
   "source_id": "psoglav-s2",
   "quote": "Псоглав је демон из српске митологије. Веровање у ова створења је било распрострањено у неким деловима Босне и у Црној Гори.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Psoglav adalah iblis dari mitologi Serbia; kepercayaan kepadanya tersebar di sebagian Bosnia dan Montenegro.",
    "en": "The psoglav is a demon of Serbian mythology; belief in it was widespread in parts of Bosnia and Montenegro."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "psoglav-c06",
   "source_id": "psoglav-s2",
   "quote": "Прождирали су људе, а чак су откопавали и гробове како би се хранили њиховим лешевима.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Mereka melahap manusia dan bahkan menggali kuburan untuk memakan mayat.",
    "en": "They devoured people and even dug up graves to feed on corpses."
   }
  }
 ],

 "short_description": {
  "id": "Nyonya Gunung Tembaga adalah makhluk legendaris para penambang Ural, penguasa Pegunungan Ural, pelindung penambang dan pemilik kekayaan bawah tanah yang tersembunyi.",
  "en": "The Mistress of the Copper Mountain is a legendary being of the Ural miners, mistress of the Ural Mountains, patroness of miners and owner of hidden underground riches.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "the-mistress-of-the-copper-mountain-c01",
   "the-mistress-of-the-copper-mountain-c02"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Nyonya Gunung Tembaga, juga disebut Gadis Malakit (Malakhitnitsa), adalah makhluk legendaris para penambang Ural, penguasa Pegunungan Ural di Rusia dan tokoh mitologi Slavia serta dongeng Rusia. Dalam cerita ia tampil sebagai perempuan muda sangat cantik bermata hijau bergaun malakit, atau sebagai kadal. Ia dianggap pelindung penambang dan pemilik kekayaan bawah tanah yang dapat mengizinkan atau mencegah penambangan di tempat tertentu.",
   "en": "The Mistress of the Copper Mountain, also the Malachite Maid (Malakhitnitsa), is a legendary being of the Ural miners, ruler of Russia's Ural Mountains and a figure of Slavic myth and Russian tales. Tales show her either as a lizard or as a lovely young woman with green eyes, dressed in malachite. Miners saw her as their patroness and as owner of hidden underground wealth who could allow or forbid mining in particular places.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "the-mistress-of-the-copper-mountain-c01",
    "the-mistress-of-the-copper-mountain-c02"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "\"Gunung Tembaga\" adalah tambang Gumyoshevsky, tambang tertua di Ural, kini di kota Polevskoy. Ia menjadi terkenal lewat kumpulan kisah Ural karya Pavel Bazhov, Kotak Malakit. Ia memerintah kadal-kadal gunung dan dapat menjelma kadal atau ular. Orang yang melihatnya jatuh di bawah pengaruhnya; ia baik kepada orang baik dan pengrajin terampil, membantu mereka menemukan permata dan emas, tetapi bila syaratnya dilanggar orang itu kehilangan keberuntungan dan keterampilan, bahkan bisa mati.",
   "en": "\"The Copper Mountain\" is the Gumyoshevsky mine, the oldest in the Urals, now in the town of Polevskoy. She became famous through Pavel Bazhov's collection of Ural tales, The Malachite Box. She commands the mountain lizards and can take the form of a lizard or a snake. Whoever sees her falls under her spell; she is kind to good people and skilled craftsmen, helping them find gems and gold, but if her conditions are broken the person loses all luck and skill and may even die.",

 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Vourdalak adalah sejenis vampir yang berasal dari sastra Rusia. Sebagian sumber Barat menyebutnya \"vampir Rusia\" yang harus meminum darah orang-orang yang dicintainya dan mengubah seluruh keluarganya; gagasan ini tampaknya bersumber dari novela Alexey K. Tolstoy, Keluarga Vourdalak. Vampir ini cenderung menyerang anggota keluarganya lebih dulu, meminum darah mereka dan menjadikan mereka vourdalak pula.",
   "en": "The vourdalak is a kind of vampire that originates in Russian literature. Some Western sources call it a \"Russian vampire\" that must drink the blood of its loved ones and convert its whole family; the notion apparently rests on Alexey K. Tolstoy's novella The Family of the Vourdalak. This vampire tends to attack members of its own family first, drinking their blood and turning them into vourdalaks in turn.",

 "short_description": {
  "id": "Azhdahak adalah raja-naga dalam mitologi Armenia, bentuk Armenia dari nama iblis Avesta Azhi Dahaka.",
  "en": "Azhdahak is a dragon-king of Armenian myth whose name is the Armenian version of Azhi Dahaka, a demon of the Avesta.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "azhdahak-mythology-c01",

 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Azhdahak adalah bentuk Armenia dari nama iblis Avesta Azhi Dahaka, dan dalam mitologi Armenia ia raja-naga, manusia-vishap. Para vishap, golongan naga tempat Azhdahak berada, hidup di gunung tinggi, danau besar, langit, dan awan; ketika naik ke langit atau turun ke danau, raungan mereka menakutkan penduduk. Gerakan azhdahak di bawah tanah dipercaya menyebabkan gempa, dan vishap yang hidup sampai seribu tahun dapat menelan seluruh dunia.",
   "en": "Azhdahak takes his name from Azhi Dahaka, a demon of the Avesta, and in Armenian myth he is a dragon-king, a man-vishap. The vishaps, the class of dragons Azhdahak belongs to, live in high mountains, great lakes, the sky and clouds; when they rise to the sky or come down to lakes, their roar terrifies people. The movement of azhdahaks underground was believed to cause earthquakes, and a vishap that lives to a thousand years can swallow the whole world.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "azhdahak-mythology-c01",
    "azhdahak-mythology-c04",
    "azhdahak-mythology-c05",
    "azhdahak-mythology-c07",
    "azhdahak-mythology-c09"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Dalam mitologi Iran ia dipenjara di Gunung Damavand dan pada akhir zaman akan lepas, lalu akhirnya dikalahkan Fereydun. Dalam Sejarah Armenia, Movses Khorenatsi menyamakan Azhdahak dengan raja Media, Astyages; dalam epos Armenia kuno Vipasank ia raja bangsa Mar (Media) yang dikalahkan raja Armenia Tigran I.
