```json
 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "azhdahak-mythology-c01",
   "source_id": "azhdahak-mythology-s1",
   "quote": "is the Armenian form of the name of the Avestan demon Azhi Dahaka. In Iranian mythology, he is imprisoned in Mount Damavand, but will at the end of time break out, to be ultimately defeated by Fereydun.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Azhdahak adalah bentuk Armenia dari nama iblis Avesta Azhi Dahaka; dalam mitologi Iran ia dipenjara di Gunung Damavand, tetapi pada akhir zaman akan lepas dan akhirnya dikalahkan Fereydun.",
    "en": "Azhdahak is the Armenian form of the Avestan demon Azhi Dahaka's name; in Iranian myth he is imprisoned in Mount Damavand but will break out at the end of time, finally to be defeated by Fereydun."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "azhdahak-mythology-c02",
   "source_id": "azhdahak-mythology-s1",
   "quote": "In his History of Armenia, the Armenian writer Movses Khorenatsi identified Azhdahak with the Median king Astyages",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam Sejarah Armenia (History of Armenia), penulis Armenia Movses Khorenatsi menyamakan Azhdahak dengan raja Media, Astyages.",
    "en": "In his History of Armenia, the Armenian writer Movses Khorenatsi identified Azhdahak with the Median king Astyages."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "azhdahak-mythology-c03",
   "source_id": "azhdahak-mythology-s1",
   "quote": "The Armenian form Azhdahak was borrowed from Parthian",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Bentuk Armenia Azhdahak dipinjam dari bahasa Parthia.",
    "en": "The Armenian form Azhdahak was borrowed from Parthian."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "azhdahak-mythology-c04",
   "source_id": "azhdahak-mythology-s2",
   "quote": "est un roi-dragon, personnage de la mythologie arménienne. Les vichaps, la catégorie de dragons à laquelle appartient Ajdahak, vivent dans les hautes montagnes, les grands lacs, le ciel et les nuages.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ajdahak adalah raja-naga dalam mitologi Armenia; para vishap (vichaps), golongan naga tempat Ajdahak berada, hidup di gunung tinggi, danau besar, langit, dan awan.",
    "en": "Ajdahak is a dragon-king of Armenian mythology; the vishaps, the class of dragons he belongs to, live in high mountains, great lakes, the sky and clouds."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "azhdahak-mythology-c05",
   "source_id": "azhdahak-mythology-s2",
   "quote": "Lorsqu'ils montent vers le ciel ou qu'ils en descendent, notamment en direction de lacs, leur rugissement provoque l'effroi des populations.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ketika naik ke langit atau turun darinya, terutama menuju danau, raungan mereka menakutkan penduduk.",
    "en": "When they rise to the sky or come down from it, especially towards lakes, their roar terrifies the people."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "azhdahak-mythology-c06",
   "source_id": "azhdahak-mythology-s2",
   "quote": "Il aurait été tué par un roi nommé Tigrane.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut Movses Khorenatsi, ia konon dibunuh oleh raja bernama Tigran (Tigrane).",
    "en": "According to Movses Khorenatsi, he was said to have been killed by a king named Tigran."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "azhdahak-mythology-c07",
   "source_id": "azhdahak-mythology-s3",
   "quote": "Движение аждааков под землёй вызывает землетрясения. Доживший до тысячи лет вишап может поглотить весь мир.",
   "locator": "В мифологии",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Gerakan para azhdahak di bawah tanah menyebabkan gempa bumi; vishap yang hidup sampai seribu tahun dapat menelan seluruh dunia.",
    "en": "The movement of azhdahaks underground causes earthquakes; a vishap that lives to a thousand years can swallow the whole world."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "azhdahak-mythology-c08",
   "source_id": "azhdahak-mythology-s3",
   "quote": "В древнеармянском эпосе «Випасанк» Аждаак был царём маров (мидийцев), которого побеждает армянский царь Тигран I Ервандид.",
   "locator": "В литературе",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam epos Armenia kuno Vipasank (Випасанк), Azhdahak adalah raja bangsa Mar (Media) yang dikalahkan raja Armenia Tigran I (Тигран I Ервандид).",
    "en": "In the ancient Armenian epic Vipasank, Azhdahak was king of the Mars (Medes), defeated by the Armenian king Tigran I Yervanduni."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "azhdahak-mythology-c09",
   "source_id": "azhdahak-mythology-s3",
   "quote": "от авест. Aži Dahāka «змеиный царь») — человек-вишап (человек-дракон) в армянской мифологии.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Nama Azhdahak (Աժդահակ) berasal dari Avesta Aži Dahāka, \"raja ular\"; ia manusia-vishap (manusia-naga) dalam mitologi Armenia.",
    "en": "Azhdahak, from Avestan Aži Dahāka, \"serpent king\", is a man-vishap (man-dragon) in Armenian mythology."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "azhdahak-mythology-c10",
   "source_id": "azhdahak-mythology-s1",
   "quote": "(Old Armenian: Աժդահակ , also spelled Ashdahak)",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam bahasa Armenia Kuno namanya Աժդահակ, juga dieja Ashdahak.",
    "en": "In Old Armenian the name is Աժդահակ, also spelled Ashdahak."
   }
  }
 ],
```

```json
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Bangpūtys adalah dewa laki-laki dalam mitologi Lituania (dan Prusia), yang direkonstruksi sebagai dewa laut, badai, angin, dan ombak.",
  "en": "Bangpūtys is a male deity of Lithuanian (and Prussian) mythology, reconstructed as a god of sea, storm, wind and waves.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "bangputys-c01",
   "bangputys-c05"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Bangpūtys adalah dewa laki-laki dalam mitologi Lituania dan Prusia, dewa badai laut, angin, dan ombak; namanya berarti \"yang meniup ombak\". Berdasarkan sumber yang sangat sedikit, sebagian ahli mitologi merekonstruksinya sebagai dewa laut dan badai yang keras dan tak kenal ampun. Menurut rekonstruksi itu ia berjanggut, bersayap, dan bermuka dua, biasanya memegang ikan di tangan kiri, perkakas di tangan kanan, dan seekor ayam jantan di kepalanya.",
   "en": "Bangpūtys is a male god of Lithuanian and Prussian mythology, god of sea storms, wind and waves; his name means \"he who blows the waves\". From very scanty sources some mythologists have reconstructed him as a stern and unrelenting god of sea and storm. In these reconstructions he is bearded, winged and two-faced, usually holding a fish in his left hand and a utensil in his right, with a rooster on his head.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "bangputys-c01",
    "bangputys-c02",
    "bangputys-c05",
    "bangputys-c06"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Anak-anaknya adalah dewa-dewa angin Rytys, Pietys, Šiaurys, dan Vakaris. Ia dianggap sangat pendendam: dalam satu kisah ia ingin menenggelamkan Auštaras, yang membuat badai saat berenang di laut. Pada abad ke-18 ia dianggap dewa laut dan dibandingkan dengan Neptunus; ia paling sering digambarkan sebagai malaikat bersayap besar, dan orang mempersembahkan jerami kepadanya agar laut tidak berbadai.",
   "en": "His sons are the wind gods Rytys, Pietys, Šiaurys and Vakaris. He is held to be very vindictive: one story tells how he wished to drown Auštaras, who made a storm while swimming in the sea. In the 18th century he was considered a sea god and compared to Neptune; he was most often shown as a great-winged angel, and people offered him straw so the sea would not storm.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "bangputys-c03",
    "bangputys-c04",
    "bangputys-c06",
    "bangputys-c07"
   ]
  }
 ],

   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Bangpūtys adalah nama dewa laki-laki dalam mitologi Lituania; berdasarkan sumber yang sangat sedikit, sebagian ahli mitologi merekonstruksinya sebagai dewa laut dan badai.",
    "en": "Bangpūtys is a male deity of Lithuanian mythology; from very scanty sources some mythologists have reconstructed him as a god of sea and storm."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "bangputys-c02",
   "source_id": "bangputys-s1",
   "quote": "According to the reconstructions, he is austere and unrelenting. He has a beard, wings and two faces. He is commonly portrayed as having a fish in his left hand, a utensil in his right hand, and a rooster on the head.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut rekonstruksi, ia keras dan tak kenal ampun, berjanggut, bersayap, dan bermuka dua, biasanya digambarkan memegang ikan di tangan kiri, perkakas di tangan kanan, dan ayam jantan di kepala.",
    "en": "According to the reconstructions he is austere and unrelenting, bearded, winged and two-faced, commonly shown with a fish in his left hand, a utensil in his right and a rooster on his head."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "bangputys-c03",
   "source_id": "bangputys-s1",
   "quote": "His sons are the gods of wind: Rytys, Pietys, Šiaurys and Vakaris (easterly, southern, northern and westerly).",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Anak-anaknya adalah dewa-dewa angin: Rytys, Pietys, Šiaurys, dan Vakaris (angin timur, selatan, utara, dan barat).",
    "en": "His sons are the wind gods Rytys, Pietys, Šiaurys and Vakaris (east, south, north and west)."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "bangputys-c04",
   "source_id": "bangputys-s1",
   "quote": "Bangpūtys is considered a very vindictive god, for example, one story talks of how Auštaras (son of Aušrinė and Mėnuo, the other god of easterly wind) was swimming in the sea and made a storm. Bangpūtys wanted to drown him.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Bangpūtys dianggap dewa yang sangat pendendam; satu kisah menceritakan Auštaras (anak Aušrinė dan Mėnuo, dewa angin timur lainnya) berenang di laut dan membuat badai, lalu Bangpūtys ingin menenggelamkannya.",
    "en": "Bangpūtys is thought very vindictive; one story tells how Auštaras (son of Aušrinė and Mėnuo, the other east wind god) swam in the sea and made a storm, and Bangpūtys wanted to drown him."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "bangputys-c05",
   "source_id": "bangputys-s2",
   "quote": "литовский и прусский бог морских бурь, штормов, ветра и волн.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Bangpūtys (Бангпутис) adalah dewa Lituania dan Prusia untuk badai laut, angin, dan ombak.",
    "en": "Bangpūtys is the Lithuanian and Prussian god of sea storms, wind and waves."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "bangputys-c06",
   "source_id": "bangputys-s2",
   "quote": "Имя означает «Веющий на волны». В XVIII веке считался богом моря и сравнивался с Нептуном.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Namanya berarti \"yang meniup ombak\"; pada abad ke-18 (XVIII) ia dianggap dewa laut dan dibandingkan dengan Neptunus.",
    "en": "His name means \"he who blows on the waves\"; in the 18th century he was considered a sea god and compared to Neptune."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "bangputys-c07",
   "source_id": "bangputys-s2",
   "quote": "Чаще всего изображался в виде большекрылого ангела. Чтобы на море не было штормов, ему обычно приносили в жертву солому.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia paling sering digambarkan sebagai malaikat bersayap besar; agar laut tidak berbadai, orang biasanya mempersembahkan jerami kepadanya.",
    "en": "He was most often shown as a great-winged angel; to keep storms off the sea, people usually offered him straw."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "bangputys-c08",
   "source_id": "bangputys-s3",
   "quote": "Vuocu etnografs Matejs Pretorejs 17 godu symteņa beiguos Bangpyuti nūsauce por vātrys i iudiņa dīvu, kam asūt bejs upurāts, pretnūstatūt tū uguņam.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Etnograf Jerman Matejs Pretorejs pada akhir abad ke-17 menyebut Bangpūtys (Bangpyuts) dewa badai dan air yang diberi persembahan, dan mempertentangkannya dengan api.",
    "en": "The German ethnographer Matejs Pretorejs in the late 17th century called Bangpūtys a god of storm and water who received offerings, contrasting him with fire."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "bangputys-c09",
   "source_id": "bangputys-s2",
   "quote": "Иногда называется другим именем Веядевис (лит. Vėjadievis). Возможно, идентичен Вейопатису.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia kadang disebut dengan nama lain Vėjadievis (Веядевис), dan mungkin identik dengan Vėjopatis (Вейопатис).",
    "en": "He is sometimes called by another name, Vėjadievis, and may be identical with Vėjopatis."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "bangputys-c10",
   "source_id": "bangputys-s4",
   "quote": "고대 프로이센어: Bangputtis )는 서부 리투아니아 신화와 프로이센 신화의 고대 바다의 신이다.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam bahasa Prusia Kuno namanya Bangputtis; ia dewa laut kuno dalam mitologi Lituania barat dan Prusia.",
    "en": "In Old Prussian his name is Bangputtis; he is an ancient sea god of western Lithuanian and Prussian mythology."
   }
  }
 ],
 "conflicts": [],
 "gaps": [
  {
   "field": "images",
   "searched": "Tidak ada gambar Commons yang terbukti menggambarkan Bangpūtys."
  },
  {
   "field": "sources",
   "searched": "Sumber kuno sangat sedikit; sebagian besar gambaran adalah rekonstruksi modern."
  }
 ]
}
```

```json
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Božić adalah tokoh dalam kepercayaan Slavia Selatan yang melambangkan tahun baru, musim semi, dan masa muda; namanya kini menjadi kata untuk Natal.",
  "en": "Božić is a figure of South Slavic belief symbolising the new year, spring and youth; his name is now the word for Christmas.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "bozic-mythology-c01",
   "bozic-mythology-c02"

 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Božić dalam paganisme Slavia adalah makhluk yang melambangkan tahun baru, musim semi, dan masa muda. Ia muncul dalam adat Natal bersama rusa bertanduk emas dan babi, sebagai lawan Badnjak (tahun yang lama). Dalam nyanyian ritual koliadki lambangnya rusa bertanduk emas, dan ia menandai awal siklus matahari yang baru pada titik balik musim dingin. Ia dianggap dewa muda, biasanya putra Badnjak.",
   "en": "Božić in Slavic paganism is a being symbolising the new year, spring and youth. He appears in Christmas customs with a golden-horned deer and a pig, as the opposite of Badnjak (the old year). In the ritual koliadki songs his symbol is the golden-horned deer, and he marks the start of the new solar cycle at the winter solstice. He is considered a young god, usually Badnjak's son.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "bozic-mythology-c01",
    "bozic-mythology-c04",
    "bozic-mythology-c06"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Badnjak dibakar dalam api, sedangkan Božić membawa pembaruan matahari, kelahiran kembali musim semi, dan peremajaan alam. Ia dianggap perwujudan matahari yang konon mati dan lahir setiap tahun, dan mungkin gema pemujaan Svarog. Dalam bahasa Slavia Selatan kata ini beralih menjadi nama Natal, misalnya Божић dalam bahasa Serbia, Božić dalam bahasa Kroasia, dan Božič dalam bahasa Slovenia.",
   "en": "Badnjak burns in the fire while Božić brings the sun's renewal, spring's rebirth and nature's rejuvenation. He is seen as the personification of the sun that allegedly dies and is born each year, and probably as an echo of the cult of Svarog. In South Slavic languages the word passed into the name for Christmas, e.g. Serbian Божић, Croatian Božić and Slovenian Božič.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "bozic-mythology-c02",
    "bozic-mythology-c03",
    "bozic-mythology-c05"
   ]
  }
 ],

  {

   "source_id": "bozic-mythology-s1",
   "quote": "in Slavic paganism is a creature symbolizing the New Year, spring and youth. It appears in Christmas customs accompanied by a golden-horned deer and a pig, as opposed to Badnjak.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Božić dalam paganisme Slavia adalah makhluk yang melambangkan tahun baru, musim semi, dan masa muda, yang muncul dalam adat Natal bersama rusa bertanduk emas dan babi, sebagai lawan Badnjak.",
    "en": "Božić in Slavic paganism is a being symbolising the New Year, spring and youth, appearing in Christmas customs with a golden-horned deer and a pig, as opposed to Badnjak."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "bozic-mythology-c02",
   "source_id": "bozic-mythology-s1",
   "quote": "In the languages of the South Slavs, the word was transferred to the modern name of Christmas, e.g. Serbian Божић, Croatian Božić and Slovenian Božič.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {

    "en": "In South Slavic languages the word passed into the modern name for Christmas, e.g. Serbian Божић, Croatian Božić and Slovenian Božič."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "bozic-mythology-c03",
   "source_id": "bozic-mythology-s1",
   "quote": "The figure of Bozić is probably an echo of the cult of Svarog. Bozić is the personification of the sun, which allegedly dies and is born every year",

   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {

    "en": "The figure of Božić is probably an echo of the cult of Svarog; Božić is the personification of the sun, which allegedly dies and is born every year."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "bozic-mythology-c04",
   "source_id": "bozic-mythology-s2",
   "quote": "Il est mentionné dans les koliadki (chants rituels). Son symbole est un cerf aux bois d'or",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia disebut dalam koliadki (nyanyian ritual), dan lambangnya rusa bertanduk emas; ia menandai awal siklus matahari yang baru pada titik balik musim dingin.",
    "en": "He is mentioned in the koliadki (ritual songs), and his symbol is a golden-antlered deer; he marks the start of the new solar cycle at the winter solstice."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "bozic-mythology-c05",
   "source_id": "bozic-mythology-s2",
   "quote": "Badniak (l'année passée) brûle dans le feu alors que Bojitch (nouvel an) apporte le renouveau du soleil, la renaissance du printemps, le rajeunissement de la nature",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Badnjak (Badniak, tahun yang lalu) terbakar dalam api, sedangkan Božić (tahun baru) membawa pembaruan matahari, kelahiran kembali musim semi, dan peremajaan alam.",
    "en": "Badnjak (the past year) burns in the fire while Božić (the new year) brings the renewal of the sun, the rebirth of spring and the rejuvenation of nature."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "bozic-mythology-c06",
   "source_id": "bozic-mythology-s3",
   "quote": "В противоположность Бадняку (старому году), Божич считается молодым богом (как правило, сыном Бадняка) и соотносится с молодостью, Рождеством и новым годом.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Berlawanan dengan Badnjak (Бадняк, tahun yang lama), Božić (Божич) dianggap dewa muda, biasanya putra Badnjak, yang dikaitkan dengan masa muda, Natal, dan tahun baru.",
    "en": "In contrast to Badnjak (the old year), Božić is considered a young god, usually Badnjak's son, linked with youth, Christmas and the new year."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "bozic-mythology-c07",
   "source_id": "bozic-mythology-s3",
   "quote": "Происхождение имени Божич из семантики — это патроним (отчество), обозначающий «сына бога»",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dari segi makna, nama Božić (Божич) adalah patronim yang berarti \"putra dewa\".",
    "en": "Semantically, the name Božić is a patronymic meaning \"son of god\"."
   }
  },
  {

   "source_id": "bozic-mythology-s4",
   "quote": "Postać Bożicza jest prawdopodobnie echem kultu Swarożyca.",
```

```json
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Cikavac adalah makhluk mitos dalam mitologi Serbia, dibayangkan sebagai burung berparuh panjang dengan kantong seperti pelikan, yang mencuri madu dan susu untuk pemiliknya.",
  "en": "The cikavac is a mythical creature of Serbian mythology, imagined as a long-beaked bird with a pelican-like pouch that steals honey and milk for its owner.",
  "claim_ids": [

 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Cikavac adalah makhluk mitos dalam mitologi Serbia, dibayangkan sebagai hewan bersayap (burung) dengan paruh panjang dan kantong seperti pelikan. Seekor cikavac dapat diperoleh dengan mengambil telur ayam betina hitam, yang lalu dibawa seorang perempuan di bawah ketiaknya selama 40 hari; selama itu ia tidak boleh mengaku dosa, memotong kuku, membasuh muka, atau berdoa.",
   "en": "The cikavac is a mythical creature of Serbian mythology, imagined as a winged animal (a bird) with a long beak and a pelican-like pouch. A cikavac could be had by taking an egg from a black hen, which a woman then carried under her armpit for 40 days, during which she must not confess, cut her nails, wash her face or pray.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "cikavac-c01",
    "cikavac-c02",
    "cikavac-c05"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Setelah menetas, cikavac mengisap madu dari sarang lebah orang lain dan susu dari ternak orang lain, lalu membawanya kepada pemiliknya. Ia memenuhi setiap keinginan pemiliknya dan membuat pemiliknya mengerti bahasa hewan. Di Republik Ceko, plivník adalah makhluk kecil yang memainkan peran serupa.",
   "en": "Once hatched, the cikavac sucked honey from other people's beehives and milk from other people's cattle and brought it to its owner. It fulfilled the owner's every wish and let the owner understand the language of animals. In the Czech Republic the plivník is a sprite that plays the same role.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "cikavac-c03",
    "cikavac-c04",
    "cikavac-c06"
   ]
  }
 ],
```

```json
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Iblis Boruta adalah tokoh iblis dalam mitologi, cerita rakyat, dan sastra Polandia yang dikaitkan dengan kota Łęczyca, perubahan wujud leshy pagan Slavia setelah Kristenisasi.",
  "en": "Devil Boruta is a devil figure of Polish mythology, folklore and literature associated with the town of Łęczyca, a post-Christian transformation of the pagan Slavic leshy.",

 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Iblis Boruta adalah tokoh dari mitologi, cerita rakyat, dan sastra Polandia yang dikaitkan dengan kota Łęczyca; ia dikatakan tinggal di ruang bawah tanah kastel Łęczyca. Tokoh ini merupakan perubahan wujud leshy pagan Slavia pada masa setelah Kristenisasi, dan juga disebut błotnik, roh rawa. Linde mendefinisikannya sebagai roh jahat yang menurut dongeng petani tinggal di rawa dan tempat berlumpur.",
   "en": "Devil Boruta is a figure of Polish mythology, folklore and literature tied to the town of Łęczyca; he is said to live in the vaults beneath Łęczyca castle. The figure is a post-Christianisation transformation of the pagan Slavic leshy, and is also called błotnik, a swamp spirit. Linde defined him as an evil spirit that, by peasant tales, lives in swamps and muddy places.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "devil-boruta-c01",
    "devil-boruta-c02",
    "devil-boruta-c03",
    "devil-boruta-c10"

  {
   "id": "Ia biasanya dianggap bangsawan yang sibuk merusak para bangsawan, digambarkan sebagai szlachcic tinggi berkumis hitam panjang dengan kontusz yang menutupi ekornya dan topi yang menutupi tanduknya. Namun ia juga muncul sebagai burung bersayap besar, ikan bertanduk, kuda hitam, burung hantu, serigala, dan penggiling. Dalam semua legenda ia sangat licik dan bertenaga melebihi manusia, tetapi dalam banyak legenda ia juga dermawan yang membagikan tepung kepada orang miskin. Zygmunt Gloger dan sejumlah etnolog menganggap Boruta berpakaian bangsawan sebagai folklor palsu abad ke-19.",
   "en": "He was usually thought of as a nobleman busy corrupting nobles, shown as a tall szlachcic with long black moustaches, a kontusz hiding his tail and a cap hiding his horns. Yet he also appeared as a great-winged bird, a horned fish, a black horse, an owl, a wolf and a miller. In all legends he is very cunning and superhumanly strong, but in many he is also a benefactor who gives flour to the poor.   {

   "source_id": "devil-boruta-s2",
   "quote": "Diabeł Boruta, zwany też błotnikiem – fikcyjna postać, diabeł zamieszkujący podziemia zamku w Łęczycy.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Iblis Boruta, juga disebut błotnik, adalah tokoh rekaan, iblis yang tinggal di ruang bawah tanah kastel di Łęczyca.",

  {
   "id": "devil-boruta-c04",
   "source_id": "devil-boruta-s1",
   "quote": "He was usually considered to be a nobleman, and accordingly, he was usually busy with corrupting nobles, leaving other social classes to other devils - like Rokita the devil from the same region, who more often tempted peasants.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia biasanya dianggap bangsawan dan sibuk merusak para bangsawan, menyerahkan golongan lain kepada iblis lain, seperti Rokita dari daerah yang sama yang lebih sering menggoda petani.",
    "en": "He was usually considered a nobleman, busy corrupting nobles and leaving other classes to other devils, like Rokita of the same region, who more often tempted peasants."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "devil-boruta-c05",
   "source_id": "devil-boruta-s2",
   "quote": "Był to wysoki szlachcic z długimi, czarnymi wąsiskami, o czarnych oczach, ubrany w bogaty kontusz zakrywający ogon i czapkę zasłaniającą rogi.",
   "locator": "Wygląd i charakter",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia digambarkan sebagai szlachcic (bangsawan) tinggi dengan kumis hitam panjang dan mata hitam, mengenakan kontusz mewah yang menutupi ekor dan topi yang menutupi tanduk; ia juga muncul sebagai penggiling (młynarz) yang mendatangi kincir pada malam hari.",
    "en": "He was a tall nobleman with long black moustaches and black eyes, wearing a rich kontusz that hid his tail and a cap that hid his horns."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "devil-boruta-c06",
   "source_id": "devil-boruta-s2",
   "quote": "jako ptak z ogromnymi skrzydłami (Boruta błotny – Błotnik) spotykany był na podłęczyckich łąkach i moczarach,",
   "locator": "Wygląd i charakter",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia juga terlihat sebagai burung bersayap besar (Boruta błotny) di padang dan rawa sekitar Łęczyca, juga sebagai ikan bertanduk, kuda hitam, burung hantu (sowa), dan serigala (wilk).",
    "en": "He was also met as a bird with huge wings (Boruta błotny) in the meadows and marshes near Łęczyca."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "devil-boruta-c07",
   "source_id": "devil-boruta-s2",
   "quote": "Obecność tego diabła mógł też potwierdzać nagle zapalający się i szybko gasnący ogień. We wszystkich legendach poświęconych diabłu Borucie wykazuje się on dużą przebiegłością, sprytem oraz nadludzką siłą.",
   "locator": "Wygląd i charakter",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kehadiran iblis ini dapat ditandai api yang tiba-tiba menyala lalu cepat padam; dalam semua legenda Boruta menunjukkan kelicikan, kecerdikan, dan kekuatan melebihi manusia, walau dalam banyak legenda ia juga dermawan bagi orang miskin.",
    "en": "His presence could be shown by a fire that suddenly flares and quickly dies; in all legends Boruta shows great cunning, wit and superhuman strength."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "devil-boruta-c08",
   "source_id": "devil-boruta-s1",
   "quote": "Zygmunt Gloger and some other ethnologists believed that dressing Boruta as a nobleman is fakelore produced in the 19th century and reintroduced into folklore.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Zygmunt Gloger dan sejumlah etnolog lain menganggap gambaran Boruta berpakaian bangsawan sebagai folklor palsu abad ke-19 yang dimasukkan kembali ke cerita rakyat.",
    "en": "Zygmunt Gloger and some other ethnologists held that dressing Boruta as a nobleman is 19th-century fakelore reintroduced into folklore."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "devil-boruta-c09",
   "source_id": "devil-boruta-s1",
   "quote": "Stories about Boruta, who was busy scaring people at night on dams and in forests and playing mischief by leading them astray, and who sometimes appeared in the form of a black dog, circulated throughout the country",
   "locator": "In Encyklopedia staropolska",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kisah tentang Boruta, yang sibuk menakuti orang pada malam hari di bendungan dan hutan, menyesatkan mereka, dan kadang muncul sebagai anjing hitam, beredar di seluruh negeri.",
    "en": "Stories of Boruta scaring people at night on dams and in forests, leading them astray and sometimes appearing as a black dog, circulated throughout the country."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "devil-boruta-c10",
   "source_id": "devil-boruta-s1",
   "quote": "\"The devil borowiec [forest spirit], błotnik [swamp spirit], an evil spirit, according to peasant fairy tales, living in swamps and muddy places.\"",
   "locator": "In Encyklopedia staropolska",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Linde mendefinisikan kata ini sebagai \"iblis borowiec (roh hutan), błotnik (roh rawa), roh jahat yang menurut dongeng petani tinggal di rawa dan tempat berlumpur\".",
    "en": "Linde defined the word as \"the devil borowiec (forest spirit), błotnik (swamp spirit), an evil spirit living, by peasant tales, in swamps and muddy places\"."
   }
  }
 ],
 "conflicts": [
  {
   "topic": {
    "id": "Boruta sebagai bangsawan",
    "en": "Boruta as a nobleman"
   },
   "positions": [
    {
     "summary": {
      "id": "Boruta biasanya dianggap bangsawan yang merusak para bangsawan.",
      "en": "Boruta was usually thought a nobleman who corrupted nobles."
     },
     "claim_ids": [
      "devil-boruta-c04"
     ]
    },
    {
     "summary": {
      "id": "Gloger menganggap gambaran itu folklor palsu buatan penulis abad ke-19.",
      "en": "Gloger considered that image fakelore made by 19th-century writers."
     },
     "claim_ids": [

    }
   ]

 ],

  {

  }
 ]
}
```

```json
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Karzełek atau Skarbnik adalah roh dalam kepercayaan Slavia yang tinggal di tambang dan lorong bawah tanah, penjaga permata, kristal, dan logam mulia.",
  "en": "Karzełek or Skarbnik is a spirit of Slavic belief living in mines and underground workings, guardian of gems, crystals and precious metals.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "karze-ek-c01",
   "karze-ek-c05"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Karzełek, juga Skarbnik (\"bendahara\"), adalah roh dalam mitologi Polandia dan kepercayaan Slavia yang tinggal di tambang dan lorong bawah tanah, penjaga permata, kristal, logam mulia, dan harta yang terkubur. Ia juga dianggap penguasa alam bawah tanah tempat ia membawa jiwa penambang yang tewas saat bekerja. Ia sering tampil sebagai penambang tua berjanggut membawa pelita, dan dapat menjelma kambing, kuda, anjing, tikus, katak, laba-laba, atau lalat, atau tetap tak terlihat sehingga hanya terdengar ketukannya.",
   "en": "Karzełek, also Skarbnik (\"treasurer\"), is a spirit of Polish mythology and Slavic belief living in mines and underground workings, guardian of gems, crystals, precious metals and buried treasure. He was also held to rule an underground realm to which he took the souls of miners killed at work. He often appeared as an old bearded miner with a lamp, and could become a goat, horse, dog, mouse, frog, spider or fly, or stay invisible so only his knocking was heard.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "karze-ek-c01",
    "karze-ek-c05",
    "karze-ek-c06",
    "karze-ek-c07"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Ia melindungi penambang dari bahaya, memperingatkan mereka akan runtuhan, banjir, dan kebakaran, menuntun yang tersesat, dan menunjukkan urat bijih. Terhadap orang jahat atau yang menghinanya ia mematikan, mendorong mereka ke jurang gelap atau meruntuhkan terowongan. Melempar batu, bersiul, atau menutup kepala dianggap menyinggungnya; ia akan memperingatkan pelaku dengan segenggam tanah sebelum bertindak lebih keras.",
   "en": "He protects miners from danger, warns them of rockbursts, floods and fires, leads the lost back and shows them veins of ore. Wicked people and those who offend him he kills, shoving them into black chasms or collapsing tunnels on top of them. Throwing rocks, whistling or covering one's head offend him; he warns the offender with handfuls of soil before acting seriously.",

 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "karze-ek-c01",
   "source_id": "karze-ek-s1",
   "quote": "is a spirit who lives in mines and underground workings and is the guardian of gems, crystals, and precious metals.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Skarbnik (dari bahasa Polandia skarbnik, \"bendahara\"), atau Kladenets (Скарбник, Кладенец), adalah roh yang tinggal di tambang dan lorong bawah tanah, penjaga permata, kristal, dan logam mulia.",
    "en": "The Skarbnik (\"treasurer\"), or Kladenets, is a spirit living in mines and underground workings, guardian of gems, crystals and precious metals."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "karze-ek-c02",
   "source_id": "karze-ek-s1",
   "quote": "It is said that he will protect miners from danger and lead them back when they are lost. He will also lead them to veins of ore.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Konon ia melindungi penambang dari bahaya, menuntun mereka kembali bila tersesat, dan menunjukkan urat bijih.",
    "en": "He is said to protect miners from danger, lead them back when lost, and lead them to veins of ore."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "karze-ek-c03",
   "source_id": "karze-ek-s1",
   "quote": "To people who are evil or insult him, he is deadly, pushing them into dark chasms or send tunnels crashing down upon them.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Terhadap orang jahat atau yang menghinanya ia mematikan: mendorong mereka ke jurang gelap atau meruntuhkan terowongan menimpa mereka.",
    "en": "To those who are evil or insult him he is deadly, pushing them into dark chasms or bringing tunnels down on them."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "karze-ek-c04",
   "source_id": "karze-ek-s1",
   "quote": "Hurling rocks, whistling, or covering one's head are actions that are offensive to Skarbnik, who will warn the offender with handfuls of pelted soil in their direction before taking serious action.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Melempar batu, bersiul, atau menutup kepala menyinggung Skarbnik, yang akan memperingatkan pelaku dengan melemparkan segenggam tanah sebelum bertindak lebih keras.",
    "en": "Throwing rocks, whistling or covering one's head offend the Skarbnik, who warns the offender with handfuls of soil before acting seriously."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "karze-ek-c05",
   "source_id": "karze-ek-s2",
   "quote": "O Karzełek (diminutivo de karzeł – um pequeno, usado para descrever um anão não-fantástico) ou Skarbnik (o Tesoureiro) na mitologia polonesa vive em minas e no funcionamento do subterrâneo.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Karzełek (diminutif dari karzeł, orang kecil atau kerdil non-fantastis) atau Skarbnik (\"Bendahara\") dalam mitologi Polandia tinggal di tambang dan lorong bawah tanah.",
    "en": "Karzełek (diminutive of karzeł, a small person or non-fantastic dwarf) or Skarbnik (\"the Treasurer\") in Polish mythology lives in mines and underground workings."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "karze-ek-c06",
   "source_id": "karze-ek-s3",
   "quote": "w wierzeniach słowiańskich duch zamieszkujący podziemia (zwłaszcza kopalnie), strzegący naturalnych zasobów ziemi i zakopanych w niej skarbów. Był także władcą podziemnej krainy, do której zabierał dusze górników, którzy zginęli podczas pracy w kopalni.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam kepercayaan Slavia, Skarbnik (дзедка dalam bahasa Belarus, Dzedka) adalah roh penghuni alam bawah tanah, terutama tambang, penjaga kekayaan alam bumi dan harta terkubur, sekaligus penguasa alam bawah tanah tempat ia membawa jiwa penambang yang tewas di tambang.",
    "en": "In Slavic belief the Skarbnik is a spirit of the underground, especially mines, guarding the earth's resources and buried treasure, and ruler of an underground realm to which he took the souls of miners killed at work."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "karze-ek-c07",
   "source_id": "karze-ek-s3",
   "quote": "Ukazywał się najczęściej pod postacią starego, brodatego górnika z kagankiem w ręku, potrafił też przybrać kształt kozy, konia, psa, myszy, żaby, pająka, muchy. Mógł także pozostawać niewidzialny, wówczas górnik mógł wyczuć jego obecność bądź słyszeć stukanie.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia paling sering tampil sebagai penambang tua berjanggut membawa pelita, dapat menjelma kambing, kuda, anjing, tikus, katak, laba-laba, atau lalat, dan dapat tetap tak terlihat sehingga penambang hanya merasakan kehadirannya atau mendengar ketukan; ia juga memperingatkan penambang akan runtuhan, banjir, dan kebakaran.",
    "en": "He mostly appeared as an old bearded miner with a lamp, could take the shape of a goat, horse, dog, mouse, frog, spider or fly, and could stay invisible, when a miner might sense him or hear knocking."
   }
  }
 ],
```

```json
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Muma Pădurii (\"ibu hutan\") adalah roh hutan dalam cerita rakyat Rumania berwujud perempuan tua buruk rupa, pelindung hewan dan tumbuhan yang juga menculik anak-anak.",
  "en": "Muma Pădurii (\"mother of the forest\") is a forest spirit of Romanian folklore in the form of an ugly old woman, protector of animals and plants who also kidnaps children.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "muma-padurii-c01",
   "muma-padurii-c03",
   "muma-padurii-c06"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Rumania, Muma Pădurii adalah perempuan tua buruk rupa yang nakal atau gila, tinggal di jantung hutan perawan dalam pondok atau pohon tua. Ia lawan peri seperti Zână, tetapi juga pelindung hewan dan tumbuhan: meracik ramuan, menolong hewan terluka, menyembuhkan hutan yang sekarat, dan mengusir penyusup dengan membuat mereka gila dan ketakutan. Ia makhluk netral yang hanya mencelakai orang yang merusak hutan.",
   "en": "Romanian folklore pictures Muma Pădurii as a hideous old woman, mischievous or mad, who dwells deep in untouched woodland in a hut or an ancient tree. Fairies like the Zână are her opposites, yet she also protects animals and plants: brewing potions, helping injured animals, healing a dying forest and driving off trespassers by maddening and frightening them. She is a neutral being, harming only those who harm the forest.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "muma-padurii-c01",
    "muma-padurii-c02"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Ia roh hutan dalam tubuh perempuan tua yang sangat buruk rupa, kadang dapat berubah wujud, dan tinggal di rumah kecil yang gelap dan tersembunyi. Ia dipercaya menyerang anak-anak sehingga banyak mantra (descântece) dipakai melawannya; dalam satu kisah populer ia hendak merebus seorang gadis kecil hidup-hidup, tetapi kakak gadis itu mengakalinya dan mendorongnya ke dalam tungku, mirip kisah Hansel dan Gretel. Orang Rumania kadang berkata \"ia mirip Muma Pădurii\" untuk menyebut seseorang jelek.",
   "en": "She is a forest spirit in the body of a very ugly old woman, sometimes able to change shape, living in a dark, hidden little house. She is thought to attack children, so many spells (descântece) are used against her; in one popular tale she tries to boil a little girl alive, but the girl's brother outwits her and pushes her into the oven, like Hansel and Gretel. Romanians sometimes say \"she looks like Muma Pădurii\" to call someone ugly.",

 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "muma-padurii-c01",
   "source_id": "muma-padurii-s1",
   "quote": "is an ugly and mischievous or mad old woman living in the forest (in the heart of virgin forests, in a hut/cabin, or an old tree). She is the opposite of fairies such as Zână. She is also the protector of the animals and plants, brewing potions and helping injured animals.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Rumania, Muma Pădurii adalah perempuan tua buruk rupa yang nakal atau gila, tinggal di hutan (jantung hutan perawan, pondok, atau pohon tua), lawan peri seperti Zână, sekaligus pelindung hewan dan tumbuhan yang meracik ramuan dan menolong hewan terluka.",
    "en": "In Romanian folklore Muma Pădurii is an ugly, mischievous or mad old woman living in the forest (deep virgin forest, a hut or an old tree), the opposite of fairies such as the Zână, and protector of animals and plants who brews potions and helps injured animals."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "muma-padurii-c02",
   "source_id": "muma-padurii-s1",
   "quote": "She cures the forest if it is dying and she keeps unwanted trespassers away by driving them mad and scaring them.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia menyembuhkan hutan yang sekarat dan mengusir penyusup dengan membuat mereka gila dan ketakutan; ia makhluk netral yang hanya mencelakai orang yang merusak hutan.",
    "en": "She cures a dying forest and keeps trespassers away by driving them mad and scaring them."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "muma-padurii-c03",
   "source_id": "muma-padurii-s1",
   "quote": "Muma Pădurii literally means \"mother of the forest\", though \"mumă\" is an archaic version of \"mamă\" (mother), which has a fairy tale overtone for the Romanian reader",
   "locator": "Etymology",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Muma Pădurii harfiah berarti \"ibu hutan\"; \"mumă\" adalah bentuk kuno dari \"mamă\" (ibu) yang bernuansa dongeng bagi pembaca Rumania.",
    "en": "Muma Pădurii literally means \"mother of the forest\"; \"mumă\" is an archaic form of \"mamă\" (mother) with a fairy-tale overtone for Romanian readers."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "muma-padurii-c04",
   "source_id": "muma-padurii-s2",
   "quote": "Mumă Pădurii es un espíritu del bosque en el cuerpo de una mujer muy fea y anciana. A veces tiene la habilidad de cambiar de forma. Suele vivir en una casita oscura, terrible y escondida.",
   "locator": "Características",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Muma Pădurii adalah roh hutan dalam tubuh perempuan yang sangat buruk rupa dan tua; kadang ia dapat berubah wujud, dan biasanya tinggal di rumah kecil yang gelap, mengerikan, dan tersembunyi.",
    "en": "Muma Pădurii is a forest spirit in the body of a very ugly old woman; she can sometimes change shape and usually lives in a dark, dreadful, hidden little house."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "muma-padurii-c05",
   "source_id": "muma-padurii-s2",
   "quote": "Se cree que ataca a los niños y debido a esto se utilizan una gran variedad de hechizos (descântece en rumano) contra ella.",
   "locator": "Características",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia dipercaya menyerang anak-anak, sehingga banyak mantra (descântece dalam bahasa Rumania) dipakai untuk melawannya.",
    "en": "She is believed to attack children, so a great variety of spells (descântece in Romanian) are used against her."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "muma-padurii-c06",
   "source_id": "muma-padurii-s2",
   "quote": "En una de las historias populares, intenta hervir viva a una niña en un caldero. Sin embargo, el hermano de la niña es más astuto que Mumă Pădurii y empuja a la mujer-monstruo al horno, como en el cuento de Hansel y Gretel.",
   "locator": "Características",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam satu kisah populer ia hendak merebus seorang gadis kecil hidup-hidup dalam kuali, tetapi kakak gadis itu mengakali Muma Pădurii dan mendorongnya ke dalam tungku, seperti kisah Hansel dan Gretel (Hansel y Gretel).",
    "en": "In one popular tale she tries to boil a little girl alive in a cauldron, but the girl's brother outwits Muma Pădurii and pushes her into the oven, as in Hansel and Gretel."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "muma-padurii-c07",
   "source_id": "muma-padurii-s1",
   "quote": "Instead of saying \"She's ugly\", Romanians sometimes say \"She looks like Muma Pădurii\".",
   "locator": "In modern culture",
   "context": "modern-popular-culture",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Alih-alih berkata \"ia jelek\", orang Rumania kadang berkata \"ia mirip Muma Pădurii\".",
    "en": "Instead of \"she's ugly\", Romanians sometimes say \"she looks like Muma Pădurii\"."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "muma-padurii-c08",
   "source_id": "muma-padurii-s3",
   "quote": "Muma Pădurii pode ser comparada à outras bruxas de folclores diferentes como a bruxa má de João e Maria dos contos dos irmãos Grimm na Alemanha (antiga Prússia), ou à Baba Yaga do folclore russo",
   "locator": "Descrição",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Muma Pădurii dapat dibandingkan dengan penyihir dari cerita rakyat lain, seperti penyihir jahat dalam kisah Hansel dan Gretel (João e Maria) dari Grimm di Jerman (Alemanha), atau Baba Yaga dari cerita rakyat Rusia.",
    "en": "Muma Pădurii can be compared with witches of other folklores, such as the wicked witch of the Grimms' Hansel and Gretel in Germany, or Baba Yaga of Russian folklore."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "muma-padurii-c09",
   "source_id": "muma-padurii-s3",
   "quote": "Muma Pădurii (em tradução livre \"Mãe da Floresta\" em romeno, também conhecida como Pădureanca ou Muma Huciului)",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Muma Pădurii (\"Ibu Hutan\") juga dikenal sebagai Pădureanca atau Muma Huciului.",
    "en": "Muma Pădurii (\"Mother of the Forest\") is also known as Pădureanca or Muma Huciului."
   }
  }
 ],
```

```json
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Ochokochi (\"manusia-kambing\") adalah makhluk setengah manusia setengah kambing dalam mitologi Georgia, khususnya Mingrelia, yang menyerang orang di hutan dan mengejar ratu hutan Tkashi-Mapa.",
  "en": "Ochokochi (\"goat-man\") is a half-man, half-goat being of Georgian, especially Mingrelian, mythology who attacks people in the forest and chases the forest queen Tkashi-Mapa.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "ocho-kochi-c01",
   "ocho-kochi-c04"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Ochokochi, harfiah \"manusia-kambing\", adalah tokoh mitologi Georgia, terutama kelompok Kolkhis dan Mingrelia: makhluk setengah manusia setengah kambing, dewa hutan jahat yang selalu berkonflik dengan pemburu dan pengumpul. Ia digambarkan raksasa berbulu merah karat dengan cakar panjang tajam, dan alih-alih bulu di dada ia memiliki tonjolan berbentuk tulang runcing atau kapak batu, yang dipakainya membelah lawan menjadi dua. Ia menyerang orang yang lewat dan membunuh mereka dengan memeluknya.",
   "en": "Ochokochi, literally \"goat-man\", is a figure of Georgian, especially Colchian and Mingrelian, mythology: a half-man, half-goat evil forest deity in constant conflict with hunters and gatherers. He is pictured as a huge monster with rust-red fur and long sharp claws, and instead of hair on his breast he has a pointed bone or stone-axe protrusion used to cleave enemies in two. He attacks passers-by and kills them by embracing them.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "ocho-kochi-c01",
    "ocho-kochi-c02",
    "ocho-kochi-c03"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Ia tidak dapat berbicara, tetapi suaranya yang bergemuruh membuat orang sangat ketakutan, dan ia paling dekat dengan Pan dalam mitos Yunani. Ia tergila-gila pada kecantikan ratu hutan Tkashi-Mapa dan terus mengejarnya, tetapi usahanya sering digagalkan para pemburu. Ochokochi yang dibunuh pemburu dapat hidup kembali setelah ditembak atau ditebas sekali lagi, dan sampai kini ia tokoh cerita seram untuk menakuti anak-anak nakal di Georgia Barat.",
   "en": "He cannot speak, but his rumbling voice fills people with indescribable fear, and he is closest to Pan of Greek myth. He is infatuated with the beauty of the forest queen Tkashi-Mapa and pursues her, though hunters often foil him. An Ochokochi killed by hunters could revive after a second shot or sword blow, and he remains a bogey used to scare naughty children in western Georgia.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "ocho-kochi-c04",
    "ocho-kochi-c05",
    "ocho-kochi-c06",
    "ocho-kochi-c08"
   ]
  }

  {

   "source_id": "ocho-kochi-s1",
   "quote": "is a figure from Georgian mythology, particularly the Colchian and Mingrelian ethnic groups. Instead of hair on his breast, he has a protuberance in the form of a pointed bone or a stone-axe. He attacks passers by, whom he kills by embracing them.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ochokochi (ოჩოკოჩი, harfiah \"manusia-kambing\") adalah tokoh mitologi Georgia, terutama kelompok Kolkhis (Colchian) dan Mingrelia; alih-alih bulu di dada ia memiliki tonjolan berbentuk tulang runcing atau kapak batu, dan ia menyerang orang yang lewat lalu membunuh mereka dengan memeluknya.",
    "en": "Ochokochi (\"goat-man\") is a figure of Georgian mythology, especially the Colchian and Mingrelian groups; instead of hair on his breast he has a pointed bone or stone-axe protrusion, and he attacks passers-by, killing them by embracing them."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ocho-kochi-c02",
   "source_id": "ocho-kochi-s2",
   "quote": "в грузинской мифологии получеловек-полукозёл, злое лесное божество, находящееся в постоянном конфликте с охотниками и собирателями.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam mitologi Georgia, Ochokochi (Очокочи) adalah makhluk setengah manusia setengah kambing, dewa hutan jahat yang selalu berkonflik dengan pemburu dan pengumpul.",
    "en": "In Georgian mythology Ochokochi is a half-man, half-goat evil forest deity in constant conflict with hunters and gatherers."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ocho-kochi-c03",
   "source_id": "ocho-kochi-s2",
   "quote": "Согласно преданиям, Очокочи представлялся огромным, покрытым рыжей (цвета ржавчины) шерстью чудовищем с длинными острыми когтями. Отличительной особенностью Очокочи был топорообразный горб, росший из грудной клетки, используемый им, чтобы рассекать противников надвое.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut cerita, Ochokochi digambarkan sebagai monster raksasa berbulu merah karat dengan cakar panjang tajam; cirinya tonjolan seperti kapak yang tumbuh dari dada, dipakai untuk membelah lawan menjadi dua.",
    "en": "By legend Ochokochi was a huge monster covered in rust-red fur with long sharp claws; his mark was an axe-like hump growing from his chest, used to cleave enemies in two."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ocho-kochi-c04",
   "source_id": "ocho-kochi-s1",
   "quote": "He is infatuated by the beauty of the Queen of the Forest, Tkashi-Mapa, after whom he chases. However, his attempts to catch her are often thwarted by the hunters who visit her forest.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia tergila-gila pada kecantikan Ratu Hutan, Tkashi-Mapa, dan terus mengejarnya, tetapi usahanya sering digagalkan para pemburu yang mendatangi hutannya.",
    "en": "He is infatuated with the beauty of the Queen of the Forest, Tkashi-Mapa, and chases her, but hunters visiting her forest often thwart him."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ocho-kochi-c05",
   "source_id": "ocho-kochi-s2",
   "quote": "Убитый охотниками Очокочи имел также способность оживать после повторного выстрела или удара меча.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ochokochi yang dibunuh pemburu juga dapat hidup kembali setelah tembakan atau tebasan pedang kedua.",
    "en": "An Ochokochi killed by hunters could also revive after a second shot or sword blow."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ocho-kochi-c06",
   "source_id": "ocho-kochi-s2",
   "quote": "Очокочи является наиболее распространённым персонажем детских страшилок, которыми до сих пор в Западной Грузии пугают непослушных детей .",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "modern-popular-culture",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ochokochi adalah tokoh paling umum dalam cerita seram anak-anak, yang sampai kini dipakai untuk menakuti anak-anak nakal di Georgia Barat.",
    "en": "Ochokochi is the most common figure of children's scary tales, still used in western Georgia to frighten naughty children."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ocho-kochi-c07",
   "source_id": "ocho-kochi-s3",
   "quote": "ოჩოკოჩი — მეგრულ მითოლოგიაში ნადირთმფარველი ღვთაება, ტყაშმაფას ანტაგონისტი.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam mitologi Mingrelia, Ochokochi adalah dewa pelindung binatang buruan, antagonis Tkashi-Mapa (ტყაშმაფა).",
    "en": "In Mingrelian mythology Ochokochi is a deity protecting game animals, the antagonist of Tkashi-Mapa."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ocho-kochi-c08",
   "source_id": "ocho-kochi-s2",
   "quote": "Очокочи был лишён дара речи, но звуки его рокочущего голоса повергали людей в неописуемый страх. Очокочи наиболее близок персонаж древнегреческих мифов Пан, являвшийся подобно Очокочи получеловеком-полукозлом",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ochokochi tidak dapat berbicara, tetapi suaranya yang bergemuruh membuat orang sangat ketakutan; tokoh yang paling dekat dengannya adalah Pan (Пан) dari mitos Yunani kuno, yang juga setengah manusia setengah kambing.",
    "en": "Ochokochi could not speak, but his rumbling voice filled people with indescribable fear; the closest figure to him is Pan of ancient Greek myth, likewise half-man, half-goat."
   }
  }
 ],
```

```json
   "id": "Pereplut adalah dewa atau iblis Slavia yang fungsinya tidak jelas. Ia muncul dalam daftar dewa dan iblis pada sisipan Rutenia abad ke-12 dalam Kata Santo Grigory (naskahnya sendiri dari abad ke-15), dan juga dalam Kata Santo Yohanes. Sumber itu hanya menyebut bahwa orang minum untuk menghormati Pereplut sambil berputar; karena tidak ada data lain, tidak ada yang dapat dikatakan pasti tentangnya.",
   "en": "Pereplut is a Slavic deity or demon of unclear function. He is listed among gods and demons in a Ruthenian insertion, dated to the 12th century, into the Word of St. Grigory, whose surviving manuscript is from the 15th century, and also in the Word of St. John. The source only says people drank in Pereplut's honour while turning around; lacking other data, nothing certain can be said about him.",
   "claim_ids": [

    "pereplut-c02",
    "pereplut-c05"

  {
   "id": "Namanya mungkin terdistorsi saat penyalinan naskah, dan putaran serta minum yang disebut mungkin jejak ritus magis dengan tarian dan persembahan minuman. Aleksander Brückner menganggapnya iblis takdir, dan Boris Rybakov menganggapnya variasi nama dewa kuno Simargl.",
   "en": "His name may have been distorted in copying, and the turning and drinking are probably traces of magic rites with dance and libation.
```
